| `POST /api/conversations/:id/preview/:previewId/terminal` | `{ input?, cols?, rows? }` → `{ ok: true }`. Keystrokes (at most 64 KB; 409 when the app is not running) and the terminal's size (1 to 1000 each). |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/preview/changed` | `{ messageId }` → `{ folder, mode, entry, shown }`. The app a finished turn made, from the files it added or changed where they are now (a parallel member's or the team's own worktree, by its real path): an index page belongs to the nearest folder above it, in the same working directory, with a compose file, a `Dockerfile` or a `package.json`, and any page also stands as itself when that does not run; the shallowest that runs, as `preview/target` detects it. `shown` is where it is in its working directory or worktree. An app's entry in another language (`app.py`, `main.go`, `server.js`…) stands for the nearest folder above it that runs. 404 when none runs; 409 `preview-not-ready` when the turn added an app that cannot run yet, with where it is and why (`appProblems()`). |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/preview/:previewId/tab` | → `{ url }`. A single-use address that opens the preview's page in a tab of its own, for the browser that started it (403 for another), good for a minute; at most eight waiting (429). |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/preview/:previewId/run` | `{ command? }` → `{ ok: true }`. Runs the main service again: with its own command, or the command line given (split as a shell would, without one; at most 4096 characters, one line). 409 while it starts or after a failure. |
| `GET /api/publish` | → `PublishSettingsView`: Settings → Publish to the cloud: the Render and Neon keys masked, the Render workspaces and the one chosen, the region, the Neon organizations and the one chosen, whether keys can be kept, and the published apps. |
| `PUT /api/publish/render` · `PUT /api/publish/neon` | `{ apiKey }` → `PublishSettingsView`. Checks the key (Render's `GET /owners`, Neon's `GET /users/me/organizations`) before it goes to the credential store; the only workspace or organization is chosen. 400 for a key refused, 503 without a credential store. |
| `PATCH /api/publish/render` · `PATCH /api/publish/neon` | `{ ownerId?, region? }` or `{ orgId? }` → `PublishSettingsView`: one of the workspaces or organizations the key reaches, one of `RENDER_REGIONS`. |
| `DELETE /api/publish/render` · `DELETE /api/publish/neon` | → `PublishSettingsView`. Removes the key and what it reached. |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/preview/:previewId/publish/plan` | → `PublishPlan`: what publishing the running preview would do (its kind, Render's commands, the variables with Neon's named only, the databases and their init scripts, the app published from the folder before, what Settings still needs, the gh sign-in). For the browser that started the preview, once its page answered (409 before); 409 with `publish-changed` when its files changed since it started. |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/preview/:previewId/publish` | `PublishRequest` (`{ name?, env?, database?, public: true }`) → `PublishedApp`, at once, `state: 'publishing'`. The same checks as the plan, then `public` must be `true`, Settings complete (400 with `publish-setup`), the name new and `APP_NAME`'s, at most 100 variables. 409 while that app publishes, 429 with two under way. |
| `GET /api/publish/apps/:appId` | → `PublishedApp` with the steps of its current or last publish (`repo`, `push`, `database`, `init`, `service`, `build`, Render's deploy state in `detail`). |
| `DELETE /api/publish/apps/:appId` | `?database=1` or `?forget=1` → `{ ok: true }`. Deletes its Render service (also one Render made for a try whose answer never came) and, with `database=1`, its Neon projects; the GitHub repository stays. `forget=1` removes it from the list alone. 409 while it publishes or is being unpublished, or when the current Render key does not reach its workspace. |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files?root=&path=` | → `FileListing`: the folders that can be browsed (`roots`: `cwd`, `cwd:1`… for the other working directories, plus each agent's worktree of each in parallel mode, `<agent>`, `<agent>:1`…), the selected root’s absolute `canonicalRoot`, and the entries of `path` in `root` (folders first; `.git` and links leading outside left out; at most 2,000), and whether the Files panel may change files in that root (`writable`; `readOnly`: why not, for an agent's worktree or a folder where edit mode is refused). 404 for anything outside the folder |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/preview?root=&path=` | → `FilePreview`: the text of a file (the first 512 KB), or that it is an image, a video or binary; `version` (a SHA-256 of its bytes) for a whole UTF-8 text file of at most 512 KB reached through no link, which the panel can edit |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/raw?root=&path=` | → an image or video in the folder, served like the media endpoint (anything else is a 404) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/download?root=&path=` | → any file in the folder as a download, like the message download endpoint (a folder, or anything outside or in `.git`, is a 404) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/zip/check?root=&path=` | → `ZipCheck` `{ all, unignored? }`: what downloading the folder (`path` '' = the root) as a ZIP file would hold, `{ files, bytes }`, or null when it is more than 10,000 files and folders or 1 GB; when `all` is null, `unignored` is the same for only what git does not ignore (null when that is too much too, or the folder is not in a repository). Nothing is read or zipped; 404 as for listing |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/zip?root=&path=&unignored=` | → the folder as a ZIP file, zipped as it is sent (`application/zip`, attachment, `nosniff`, sandboxed), its entries in a folder of its name; the same entries as the browser lists (no `.git`, links only to what is inside, each folder once, empty folders kept), with their permission bits and names safe for any unzip tool; an agent's worktree root is named `<working directory>-<agent>`; `unignored=1`: only the files git does not ignore (in repositories inside it too). 413 when it is too large; 404 as for listing; `HEAD` gives the headers only |
| `PUT /api/conversations/:id/files/text` | `FileSaveRequest` `{ root?, path, text, version }` → `FileWritten` `{ path, size, mtime, version }`: the text saved over the file as it was loaded (`version` from its preview), or a new file (`version: null`), written at once (a temporary file renamed over it), its permission bits, CRLF line breaks and byte order mark kept. Only in a working directory where edit mode would be allowed (403 for an agent's worktree, or a folder that holds Harness Relay, the home folder or a CLI's own folder), never through a link (403), into `.git` or a file the server withholds (404) or what Harness Relay protects (403); a read-only file 403, a folder 409. 409 `file-changed` when the file is not the version loaded (or gone), `file-exists` for a new file where one is, `folder-busy` while a run may be writing in that folder; 400 for a NUL, a lone surrogate or (new file) a name no system takes; 413 over 512 KB, or a body over its limit |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/files/upload` | `FileUploadRequest` `{ root?, dir, name, data, replace? }` → `FileWritten`: one file (base64) into the folder `dir`, at most 20 MB (413); a file of that name is replaced only with `replace: true` (409 `file-exists` otherwise). The same refusals as a save, and 400 for a name no system takes |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/diff` | → unified diff (text) of that turn's changes (in several repositories: one after another, each one's paths starting with its folder's name) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/agents/:agentId/diff` | → unified diff (text) of an agent's whole worktree result (all its worktrees, named so); `@team` (`TEAM_WORKTREE`, `%40team` in the URL) for the team's own worktrees, which it works in while another conversation changes the folder |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/agents/:agentId/apply` | `{ force?, confirm? }` (or no body) → `{ files, held? }`: applies an agent's worktree result (or with `@team`, the team's own) to the working directories (all its worktrees, every patch checked first: 409 on a conflict in any, and nothing is applied; 409 too while another conversation writes in one of them); with team checks they run first on the folders with the result in them, and one that does not pass applies nothing (`held`: its card's id) unless `force` (the card's Apply anyway, for the result that card checked; one changed since is checked again), and `confirm` runs the commands that the result's changed settings held back |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/events` | SSE stream of `ServerEvent` |
| `GET /api/presets` · `POST /api/presets` · `DELETE /api/presets/:id` | team templates (built-in ones first); `POST` takes `{ id?, name, description?, agents, settings }`, and the id of a saved template (`user-…`) updates it. A member (here, in `POST /api/conversations`, `PUT /api/conversations/:id` and `revise-team`) may have `limits` (`AgentLimits`): only its CLI's is kept (`AGENT_LIMITS`), and one out of its bounds is refused (400) |
| `POST /api/presets/draft` | `TemplateDraftRequest` (`{ request, name, description, agents, settings }`: the template editor's template; `agents` may be empty, or a new template's one on each CLI ready here, more than a team may have, which the answer keeps within the limit) → `TemplateRevision` (`agents`, `settings`, `name`, `description`, `reason`, `meta`): the lead agent writes the template as asked, read-only, in an empty folder of its own (a template has none): a new one's members (none has a role yet) are only a starting point; its members are matched as `revise-team`'s are, and a name or description it leaves out stays. Nothing is saved (400, 503 and 409 as for `revise-team`) |
| `GET /api/settings` · `PUT /api/settings` | `{ defaultCwd?, defaultExtraCwds?, createCwd?, lead?, chatTitles?, agentHome?, agentsViewUrl?, agentsViewLink?, checksDefault? }` (`chatTitles`: whether the lead agent names new conversations, a boolean, else 400; `SettingsView.chatTitles` says it, as Settings saved it or else as `RELAY_CHAT_TITLES` does; `checksDefault`: the team checks new chats and automations start with, a `CheckList` validated as a chat's, `null` or a list without rules for none, never marked `fromDefault`; `lead`: `{ provider, model?, effort?, account? }`, the auto-team lead agent, `account` one of that CLI's account ids or left out for the default one; `defaultExtraCwds`: the default working directories after `defaultCwd`, given with it (none when left out), at most eight in all, each once; ones that do not exist are a 404 with `code: "cwd-missing"` and `paths` unless `createCwd: true`, which creates them; `agentHome`: the folder of the CLIs' own homes, an absolute or `~` path, created when missing, not the home folder, a root, or inside Harness Relay's folder or a default working directory, `""` = off, 409 while a conversation runs, is sorted into a group or has its team changed by the lead agent; `agentsViewUrl`: AgentsView's address, `http(s)` on `localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `[::1]` only, saved as its origin, `""` = off; `agentsViewLink`: where the UI's **Open AgentsView** goes instead, any http(s) address, `""` = `agentsViewUrl`). The answer has `agentsViewError` while the last update of AgentsView at the current address failed |
| `PATCH /api/settings/checks-default` | `{ protectedPaths?, commitPattern?, commands?, hooks?, previewApp?, autoFix? }` → `CheckList`. Update the supplied default team check fields and return the saved list. Omitted fields keep their current values. An empty array or commit pattern clears that field, and `false` turns `previewApp` or `autoFix` off. |
| `PUT /api/settings/providers/:provider` | `{ bin?, apiKey?, sandboxTerminal?, passEnv? }` → `SettingsView`: what the CLI has once for all its accounts (its executable, and `passEnv`: the whole list of other providers' keys of the server's environment its runs are given all the same, names only, at most 32, each `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`; a 400 for an open agent), and the default account's key (`apiKey: null` removes). A request that names `defaultModel`, `defaultEffort`, `nativeOff`, `nativeItems` or `nativeOn` is a 400: those are each account's (below). `sandboxTerminal`: whether OpenHands' commands may use a terminal in the relay sandbox on macOS, ignored for the other CLIs ([Open agents → OpenHands](open-agents.md#openhands)); an open agent has no key here, its keys are the model backends'. |
| `GET /api/backends` | → `ModelBackendsView`: the open agents' model backends, `apis` (`openai`, `anthropic`, `xai`) and `endpoints`, keys masked, with where each is kept (`keyStorage`) and `keyUnreadable` for one the credential store could not give at startup, and `deletedEndpoints`: the ids of endpoints deleted and neither added again nor forgotten. An endpoint's `limits`, by model id, only for the models that have any: the context window and output limit the user set (`context`, `output`) and what the server reported at the last save that checked its models (`reported`) |
| `GET /api/labs` | → `LabsView`: Settings → Labs' mode (`off`, `code` or `rlm`, with `rlmModel`), where it comes from (`saved`, `env`: `RELAY_RUN_CODE` or `RELAY_RLM`, or `default`), and `unavailable`: why the modes cannot run on this computer (native Windows, no Node.js, Harness Relay's own sandbox not starting), in the request's language |
| `PUT /api/labs` | `{ mode, rlmModel? }` → `LabsView`: keeps the mode, over the environment's from then on; `rlm` only with `rlmModel` as `<backend>/<model>` on a model connection set up now, or as `cli:<provider>[:<account>][/<model>]` for an account the CLI has (Claude Code, Copilot or Muse Code: `SUB_MODEL_CLIS`) (else 400 with why); a model given with another mode is dropped |
| `PUT /api/backends/:id` | `{ apiKey }` → `ModelBackendsView`: sets an API backend's key in the credential store (`backend-<id>-api-key`); `null` or `""` removes it. 404 for another id |
| `POST /api/backends/endpoint-models` | `{ baseUrl, endpointId?, apiKey? }` → `{ models: string[] }`. Fetches `GET <baseUrl>/models` when the user clicks **Load models**. A supplied key goes in the Bearer authorization header. Omit it to reuse a saved key only for the same endpoint ID and normalized base URL, or send `null` for an unauthenticated request. Reads the distinct IDs in `data[].id` without saving the endpoint. The server sends one GET with a 10-second timeout, refuses redirects and limits the JSON response to 4 MiB. |
| `PUT /api/backends/endpoints/:id` | `{ name, baseUrl, models, apiKey?, verifyModels?, allowMissingMetadata?, limits? }` → `ModelBackendsView`: adds a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint, or replaces the one with this id (a lowercase slug, not `openai` / `anthropic` / `xai`; it cannot change). `baseUrl`: http(s) without credentials, query or fragment, not Harness Relay's own address; `models`: the selected or manually entered IDs, without spaces (at most 100); omitting `apiKey` keeps the key only for an unchanged base URL, `null` removes it (`endpoint-<id>-api-key`). Settings sends `verifyModels: true`. An edit that changes only the display name saves locally, including an existing endpoint with an empty model list. When adding an endpoint or changing its URL, credentials or models with this flag, the server checks every ID against a fresh `GET <baseUrl>/models`. For IDs missing from that list, or if the list route returns 404, 405 or 501, it requests `GET <baseUrl>/models/<encoded ID>` and requires a JSON object whose `id` exactly matches. When the catalog and every individual lookup return 404, 405 or 501, the response is 409 with code `endpoint-metadata-unavailable`. Settings offers to retry with `allowMissingMetadata: true` only after the user confirms. The retry rechecks the endpoint and permits only that missing-metadata outcome. Network and access failures, unknown IDs with working metadata, and ID mismatches still block the write. No inference request is made. If another request changes the endpoint during verification, the save returns 409. Omit the flag or send `false` to save without a network check. Adding one under a deleted id takes that id out of `deletedEndpoints`, in the same write. `limits`: the context windows and output limits the user set, by model id, `{ context?, output? }` in whole tokens (a window from 1,024 to 10,000,000, an output limit from 16 to 1,000,000 and less than its window), only for models in `models`; omitted, those set before are kept; Settings always sends it. A save that checks the models also reads the context window the server reports from the same answers (vLLM's `max_model_len`, llama.cpp's `meta.n_ctx`, in the catalog or a model's own record) and keeps it apart as `reported`; one that changes only the name keeps what was reported, one to another address drops it. An output limit not less than the window the server reports is refused (400). 400 for what does not fit or more than 20 endpoints |
| `DELETE /api/backends/endpoints/:id` | → `ModelBackendsView`: deletes the endpoint and its key, and remembers its id (`deleted_endpoints`): a Claude Code, Codex, Grok or Copilot model `<id>/…` then fails its turn instead of reaching the CLI's own service; 404 for an unknown one |
| `DELETE /api/backends/deleted-endpoints/:id` | → `ModelBackendsView`: forgets a deleted endpoint's id, so that models `<id>/…` go to their CLI as they are again (a model of the user's own Codex config that starts with that name, say); 404 for an id not remembered, 400 for one that is not an endpoint id |
| `POST /api/settings/providers/:provider/accounts` | `{ label }` → `{ id, settings: SettingsView }`: adds an account to the CLI, signed out and with no key (`label`: at most 40 characters on one line; empty is allowed); a Grok account starts with a copy of the default account's folder trust. 409 at the account limit (`RELAY_MAX_ACCOUNTS`, else 10, the default one included); 400 for an open agent, which has no sign-in, so no other account |
| `PUT /api/settings/providers/:provider/accounts/:account` | `{ label?, apiKey?, defaultModel?, defaultEffort?, nativeOff?, nativeItems?, nativeOn? }` → `SettingsView`: renames the account (`default` = the default one), sets its key (`null` removes it; a Codex key signs that account in with `codex login --with-api-key` and is not kept), or changes how Harness Relay runs it: its default model and effort (empty = the CLI's own, from its home), `nativeOff`: the whole list of the CLI's own parts its runs leave out, of `skills`, `mcp`, `tools`, `instructions`, `memory`, `hooks`, `agents`, `plugins` and `subagents` (its whole subagent feature; parts the CLI cannot leave out are dropped); `nativeItems`: all the single items its runs leave out, `{ <kind>: [id…] }` with kinds `skills`, `mcp`, `mcpTools`, `tools`, `instructions`, `hooks`, `agents`, `plugins` and the ids from `NativeSetup`, at most 2,000 per kind (kinds the CLI cannot leave out alone are dropped); `nativeOn`: the whole list of what its runs turn on, of `workflows` and `ultracode` (`NATIVE_ON_SUPPORT`; ultracode only with workflows; the rest dropped). Each account in `SettingsView.providers[].accounts` has these five. 404 for an account the CLI does not have |
| `DELETE /api/settings/providers/:provider/accounts/:account` | → `SettingsView`: forgets the account and its key (agents that named it, and the lead agent, use the default account from then on), then signs it out (as far as the CLI can in 15 s) and deletes its folder. 400 for `default`, 409 while a conversation runs |
| `GET /api/tools` | → `ToolLibraryView`: MCP servers (variable and header values masked; an HTTP server that signed in itself with its `oauth` state, `signed-in` with `signedInAt` or `reauth` with `error`, never a token), skills (read from their `SKILL.md` now, with a `problem` when one cannot be used), memory per scope, where secrets are kept |
| `PUT /api/tools` | `{ skills?, memory?, relayTools?, browser? }` → `ToolLibraryView`. `relayTools` gives each of Harness Relay's tool groups named to the CLIs listed; `browser` is the browser the browser tools start, a file there now (a full path, or from `~/`; `400` with why otherwise), or empty to find one by itself again (the view's `browser` says it, `RELAY_BROWSER`'s and the one in use). `skills` replaces the list (`[{ path, providers? }]`; a new path may be a folder of skills, and must hold at least one); `memory` changes the given parts (`all` or a CLI's id: `claude`, `codex`, `grok`, `cursor`, `antigravity`, `copilot`, `muse`, `opencode`, `goose`, `cline`, `openhands`, `pi`; at most 20,000 characters each) |
| `PUT /api/tools/mcp/:name` | `{ transport, command?, args?, url?, env?, headers?, providers?, previousName? }` → `ToolLibraryView`: adds a server, or with `previousName` replaces or renames one. A variable or header without `value` keeps its stored value. 409 for a taken name |
| `DELETE /api/tools/mcp/:name` | → `ToolLibraryView` (its values are removed from the credential store; its own sign-in's tokens too, and revoked) |
| `POST /api/tools/mcp/:name/signin` | `{ origin? }` → `OAuthSignInView` (`provider: "mcp"`, `server: { id, name }`): starts an HTTP server's own sign-in (MCP's authorization): discovery, a client registered at its authorization server when the kept one lacks this redirect address, then its page to open, or, when that page or the token endpoint is not on the authorization server's own origin, no page but `confirm: { host, issuer, tokenHost? }`, the page given once confirmed (`POST /api/oauth/signins/:id/confirm`); followed (`GET /api/oauth/signins/:id`), pasted (`…/address`), cancelled (`DELETE …`) and finished at `POST /api/oauth/callback` as an account's. `409` with `code: "mcp-no-sign-in"` for a server that asks for none, `502` with why for one that cannot be signed in to (no PKCE S256, no registration, metadata for another resource, another issuer, unreachable, not https, an address on this computer or the local network for a server elsewhere, a registration refused), `400` for a stdio server, `404` for none, `429` with 10 waiting or being prepared |
| `DELETE /api/tools/mcp/:name/signin` | → `ToolLibraryView`: signs the server out: its tokens deleted from the credential store and the server's memory, and revoked at its authorization server's revocation endpoint when it has one; `404` for no such server |
| `GET /api/tools/skills/scan?path=` | → `{ path, skills: SkillCandidate[] }`: the skills under a folder (the folder itself and skill folders up to two levels down, at most 300), marked when already added; nothing is saved |
| `GET /api/memory` | → `MemoryView`: long-term memory's settings (`enabled`, `review`, `autoTidy`; off, off, on by default), every note (waiting ones first, then pinned ones, then the newest), the titles of the conversations they came from (`sources`), the last tidying (`lastTidy`: when, how many changes, the lead agent's summary or why it failed), whether the lead agent is tidying now and how many notes changed since the last tidying |
| `PUT /api/memory/settings` | `{ enabled?, review?, autoTidy? }` (booleans) → `MemoryView`; turning memory or automatic tidying off stops a tidying under way |
| `POST /api/memory/notes` | `{ text, cwd?, pinned? }` → `MemoryView`: a note of the user's (`basis: user`), for every conversation or, with `cwd` (absolute, `~` expanded), for conversations with that folder; 400 for an empty one or one over 1,000 characters, 409 past 1,000 notes |
| `PATCH /api/memory/notes/:id` | `{ text?, cwd?, pinned?, state? }` → `MemoryView`: changed text makes the note the user's; `cwd: null` for everywhere; `state: "active"` keeps a waiting note (an agent's correction sets aside the notes it replaces) or restores one set aside, `"archived"` sets it aside; 404 for an unknown note |
| `DELETE /api/memory/notes/:id` | → `MemoryView`: deletes the note for good (a waiting one is discarded so); its card in the reply says so; 404 for an unknown note |
| `POST /api/memory/tidy` | → `MemoryView`: has the lead agent tidy the notes now, in the background (`tidying`); 409 while memory is off, while it tidies, or when no note is in use |
| `GET /api/providers[?refresh=1]` | → `ProviderStatus[]`, each with `accounts`: every account's sign-in, the default one first; `checkedAt`: when its check began (a cached answer keeps its check's) |
| `GET /api/providers/:provider/usage[?account=<id>][&refresh=1]` | → `AccountUsage`: how much of the account's plan is used (the default account's without `account`; 404 for one the CLI does not have), as its CLI reports it without a prompt, in that account's home: its plan, each limit (`span` or `minutes`; `kind` for what Copilot counts, or `scope` for a model's own or the CLI's own name; `usedPercent`, `resetsAt`; `used` and `total` for a limit of requests; `paidPast` for one its `payer` pays past), `planUsageBlocked` when the CLI says the plan's included usage is blocked (Codex's `ordinaryUsageAllowed: false`), the `payer` that keeps it running, billed, past its plan while it can pay now (`credits`, `usageCredits`, `additionalUsage`, `payAsYouGo`), the `credits` left (Codex's); or `none` and why (`apiKey`: an account on a key its provider bills by use, told without asking its CLI except Claude Code and Codex, which say so themselves; `signedOut`, which is also an Antigravity account without a Gemini API key; `noAllowance`; `unlimited`; `noData`; `unavailable`: Claude Code without plan limits for how the account signs in (a token from `claude setup-token`, Bedrock, Vertex); `notReported`: Cursor, which shows its usage only in its own terminal UI and gives its plan from `about` (not for an account on a key, nor another account, whose plan `about` would not describe), and an open agent); or `error` in the CLI's words, or `problem` in Harness Relay's (`timedOut`, `noAnswer`, `cliMissing`: a code the UI words, as the answer is kept for every browser whatever its language). Each CLI is asked in one empty folder, the same each time (`data/tmp/usage-probe`). Claude Code answers a `get_usage` control request (in an empty folder, without settings files, hooks or MCP servers), Codex `account/read` and `account/rateLimits/read` on `codex app-server`, Grok `_x.ai/billing` over ACP (without a session, its own setup left out), Muse Code `muse usage --json`, Copilot `account.getQuota` and `account.getCurrentAuth` on its headless server (`copilot --headless --stdio`, `Content-Length` framing; in a home of the question's own with a copy of the account's sign-in state, and a fresh cache folder); Antigravity is never asked: it runs on a Gemini API key only, so `apiKey` with a key and `signedOut` without. Kept two minutes per account; dropped, and an ask under way stopped, when the CLI's cached state is (a sign-in, key or setting changed here), when a status check finds an account signed in otherwise than the last one did, and with a removed account; `refresh=1` asks again unless an ask is under way |
| `GET /api/limits` | → `ServerLimits` `{ maxAccounts, maxTeamAgents, turnTimeoutMinutes }`: what the environment sets that the UI keeps to or shows (`RELAY_MAX_ACCOUNTS`, `RELAY_MAX_TEAM_AGENTS`, `RELAY_TURN_TIMEOUT_MIN`) |
| `GET /api/about` | → `AboutInfo` `{ version, desktop, os, node }`: which Harness Relay this is (the version the desktop app was built as, else `source (<commit>)`) and on what, for a problem report; no paths or names |
| `GET /api/update` | → `UpdateStatus` `{ current, updates, available?, progress?, checking?, checkedAt?, checkFailed? }`: this version, and what the desktop app last said of its updates (`updates: false`, with nothing else, for `npm start`) |
| `POST /api/update/check` | Has the desktop app ask GitHub now (at most every five minutes; otherwise what it knows) → `UpdateStatus` once the app has answered this check by its id (or after 35 s, longer than the app gives GitHub) |
| `POST /api/update/install` | Has the desktop app update to the newer version, as from its menu (it asks the user first, in its own dialog) → `{ ok: true }`; 409 when there is none, or one is under way |
| `PUT /api/desktop/lang` | `{ lang }` (`zh-TW` or `en`): the language the chat in the desktop app's window shows, sent by that page as it opens and when the user picks another; the app's tray menu, menu bar and dialogs follow it (`lang` message) → `{ ok: true }`, also without the app (nothing to tell); 400 for anything else; 403 unless the request's `Host` is this computer's (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`): a phone through the tailnet never changes it |
| `POST /api/report/screenshots` | `{ files: [{ data }] }` (base64, at most 5, each at most 10 MB; PNG, JPEG or WebP by their bytes, not GIF) → `ReportScreenshots` `{ id, url }`: a problem report's screenshots as one secret gist through the user's `gh`, named `screenshot-1.png`… (never their own names) with only what showing them needs (`withoutMetadata()`: no location, EXIF but the orientation, XMP, IPTC, comments, thumbnails, text, content credentials or anything after the image; a JPEG or PNG cut short after its image data began ended there, a WebP cut short refused; 400 for one it cannot read through), `url` its page on GitHub for the mail; 400 for none or one that is not an image, 413 for too many or too large, 502 for `gh`'s trouble (`code`: `gh-missing`, `gh-signed-out`) |
| `DELETE /api/report/screenshots/:id` | Deletes that gist again: only one uploaded since the server started (404 otherwise) |
| `GET /api/models[?refresh=1]` | → `ProviderModels[]`: one per account of each CLI, asked in its home (its CLI defaults are from its own config) and with its own defaults in `defaultModel` / `defaultEffort`; each CLI's default account first, with no `account`, the others with theirs (`catalogFor()` picks one). Kept for ten minutes per account; changing an account's defaults shows at once |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/native[?account=<id>]` | `{ refresh? }` → `NativeSetup`: the skills, MCP servers (names, kinds and states only) and their tools, built-in tools (Claude Code), hooks (never their commands), subagents and plugins (when the CLI lists them) and instruction and memory files the CLI loads by itself for the default working directory, in the home of the default account or of `account` (404 for one the CLI does not have), with `errors` for what it did not report. Each item the CLI can leave out alone has the `id` it is left out by. With anything left out of that account's runs, the CLI is asked again the way Harness Relay starts it as that account: `nativeOff`, `nativeItems` and `asRun` say so, and items it then leaves out have `leftOut: true`. Starts the CLI briefly without a prompt (a POST, so other sites cannot set it off; Claude Code gets a `/usage` turn it answers by itself; Cursor is not started, its lists come from the files it reads; Antigravity answers `agy -p /skills`, `/hooks` and `/agents` and `agy plugin list --json` on the account's Gemini API key, and is not asked without one (an `errors` line saying so), its MCP servers and instruction files come from the files it reads; Copilot answers `copilot mcp list --json`, `skill list --json`, `instruction list --json` and `plugin list --json`, its hooks and custom agents come from the files it reads; Muse Code answers `muse skills list --json` in a config folder of the question's own, as a read-only run would, so a project's skills, which it loads only in a workspace it trusts, are left out, and its MCP servers and hooks come from its settings; an open agent is asked through its adapter's own probe, or answers that Harness Relay cannot list what it loads yet); kept for a minute per CLI, executable, folder and account while what that account leaves out stays the same (`refresh: true` asks again) |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/native/file[?account=<id>]` | `{ path }` → `FilePreview`: one of the files the CLI reported for that account (an instruction or memory file, or a skill's `SKILL.md`, matched exactly), to read in the UI; any other path is a 404. Checked against that account's latest answer however old, so opening a file does not start the CLI again |
| `POST /api/providers/grok/folder-trust` | `{ cwd?, trust?, folder? }` → `GrokFolderTrust` `{ cwd, folder, above, trusted }`: whether Grok trusts `cwd` (default: the default working directory), from `grok inspect`. `folder` is the one Grok decides trust for (the repository root, links resolved, else `cwd`'s real path); `above` says it is a repository root above `cwd`. With `trust: true` (sent only after the user confirmed `folder`) it asks Grok again and records the trust for that folder as `grok --trust` would, in `$GROK_HOME/trusted_folders.toml` of every Grok account (all or none); 409 when Grok now names another folder than the `folder` sent, or still reports it untrusted (the file is then put back as it was) |
| `GET /api/providers/:provider/login-methods` | → `LoginMethod[]` (empty for an open agent, and for Antigravity, which runs on a Gemini API key only) |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/login[?account=]` | `{ method }` → `LoginSessionView`. Here and in the next four routes, `account` is one of the CLI's account ids (none, or `default`, = the default account; 404 for one it does not have), and each account has its own sign-in. 400 for an open agent, which has no sign-in, for every Antigravity account, the default one too, and a Cursor or Muse Code account besides the default one, which sign in with an API key only (`keyOnlyAccount()`; Antigravity has no sign-in method at all), and on Windows for a CLI that is a script Harness Relay cannot start (`WindowsScriptError`) |
| `GET /api/providers/:provider/login[?account=]` | → `LoginSessionView \| null` (poll while running) |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/login/input[?account=]` | `{ text }` → writes to the login process's stdin |
| `DELETE /api/providers/:provider/login[?account=]` | cancels the login |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/logout[?account=]` | signs the account out (400 for an open agent; an Antigravity account, the default one too, and a Cursor or Muse Code account besides the default one kept no sign-in, so nothing runs: a Google sign-in agy keeps is left as it is; each Copilot account with Copilot's ACP `logout` method, in its home; Muse Code's runs `muse logout` in its folders, which drops the key and the Meta sign-in muse keeps, not a `META_API_KEY`; Cursor's default account runs `cursor-agent logout` without its key, in the credential store the server was started with (`AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE`) if it names one, which signs out of Cursor's own sign-in) |
| `GET /api/clis` | → `CliInstallView[]`, one per CLI in `PROVIDERS` order: how Harness Relay installs it here (`method`, `command`, its folder `dir`), why it cannot (`blocked`: `npm`, `curl`, `tar`, `bash`, `gitBash`, `powershell` or `system`), the Node.js version whose build goes into the folder first when npm is not on the PATH (`node`), what it installed (`installed`: the program, its version, when, and whether the CLI's executable path is that program, `inUse`), and its install under way or the last one since the server started (`job`: state, the installer's output, its error; poll while `job.state` is `running`) |
| `POST /api/clis/:provider/install` | → `CliInstallView`: installs the CLI in the background into its folder, checks that it runs, then sets the CLI's executable path to it (409 while it installs or when Harness Relay installed it already; 400 when it cannot be installed here) |
| `DELETE /api/clis/:provider/install` | cancels the install under way (409 when none runs); what it left is removed |
| `DELETE /api/clis/:provider` | → `CliInstallView`: deletes what Harness Relay installed of the CLI and sets its executable path back to the one before the install, if it is still the program removed (404 when Harness Relay installed nothing of it; 409 while it installs or agents are at work) |
| `GET /api/sandbox-setup` | → `SandboxSetupView`: what the agents' OS sandboxes need that is missing here (`needs`: each one's `kind`, `packages`, `apparmor` or `node`, its `items`, whose sandboxes need it `for`, the `command` to run by hand, and why Harness Relay cannot install it here, `blocked`: `admin`, `packageManager`, `curl`, `tar` or `node`), how an install gets administrator rights (`admin`: `root`, `sudo` or `pkexec`), and its install under way or the last one since the server started (`job`; poll while `job.state` is `running`) |
| `POST /api/sandbox-setup/install` | → `SandboxSetupView`: installs everything missing that can be installed here, in the background (409 while one runs, 400 when nothing can be) |
| `DELETE /api/sandbox-setup/install` | → `SandboxSetupView`: stops the install under way (409 when none runs) |
| `GET /api/git-install` | → `GitInstallView` with Git availability, the install method or manual command, and the current or last installation job. Use `?bash=1` to check for Git for Windows with Git Bash. |
| `POST /api/git-install` | → `GitInstallView` after starting installation on the server host. Takes the same query option and a JSON request from a signed-in browser. Poll GET while the job runs. |
| `GET /api/voice` | → `VoiceStatus`: engine, models, selected model and language, the current or last install (`job`), the engine process (poll while `job.state` is `running`) |
| `PUT /api/voice/settings` | `{ model?, language?, bin? }` → `VoiceStatus` (`bin`: path to `whisper-server`, empty = find it) |
| `POST /api/voice/install` | `{ model? }` → `VoiceStatus`: installs what is missing (engine, model) in the background, then selects the model (409 while another install runs; 400 when it cannot be installed here or the disk is too full) |
| `DELETE /api/voice/install` | cancels the install |
| `DELETE /api/voice/models/:model` | deletes a downloaded model |
| `DELETE /api/voice` | deletes the downloaded models and engine (one installed with Homebrew stays) |
| `POST /api/voice/prepare` | starts the engine ahead of a transcription (sent when recording starts) |
| `POST /api/voice/transcribe` | `{ audio }` (base64 16 kHz mono 16-bit WAV, at most 120 s) → `{ text }` (409 when voice input is not set up) |

Create-chat and send-message requests accept an optional `requestId` (32 lowercase hex digits). Retrying the same payload and ID on the same route returns the same conversation or the original `queued` result. Reusing the ID with different content returns 409.

Types are in `src/shared/types.ts`.

### App preview runtime

`appPreviews.ts` owns the session and its credentials. `previewTarget.ts` selects a root using the conversation and reply author and detects the entry page or npm script. For a selected project it checks for a start script or default `server.js` before a build script. An HTML index linking to uncompiled modules uses its adjacent npm project. Standalone HTML and bundled assets open directly. `previewFiles.ts` reads the snapshot and serves static previews. `previewRuntime.ts` manages the two Docker modes.

Use a local Docker CLI with a Linux x64 or arm64 engine on a Unix socket or Windows named pipe. Relay pulls the digest-pinned Node 22 image when needed. Before handing it project code, Relay checks cgroup v2 limits of one CPU, 768 MiB of memory with swap disabled and 96 processes, a non-root user, seccomp and restricted privileges. The container root is read-only. Its workspace (384 MiB) and `/tmp` (32 MiB) are tmpfs mounts.

`previewDockerPackages.ts` accepts canonical tarballs in the public npm registry from a v2/v3 lockfile and checks SHA-512, up to 24 MiB per package and 96 MiB in total. A project is either a standalone npm package or a member of an npm workspace. `previewTarget.ts` finds a member's workspace root as npm does: the nearest folder above it, inside the same working directory, whose `package.json` `workspaces` patterns (globs, `{ packages }`, `!` exclusions) list it (`npmRoot`). The snapshot is then that root, and the lockfile's members, npm's links to them and the packages installed under them are checked too. Nested workspaces, Git or file dependencies, npm aliases and custom registries are unsupported. A dependency in a `package.json` that the lock's record of that package lacks, added without `npm install`, is named before any download, since npm would otherwise fail offline with `ENOTCACHED`. The container runs `npm ci --offline`, for a member with `--workspace=<its folder> --include-workspace-root`, so a sibling member's dependencies are left out. Project dependencies must install with the tools in the pinned Node image, so native modules usually need a compatible prebuilt binary.

Container previews (`previewContainer.ts`, `previewCompose.ts`, `previewEngine.ts`, `previewTar.ts`) need Docker Compose v2 and use the same engine checks. `previewCompose.ts` reads a compose file with `yaml`, interpolates it with an empty environment, and turns it into a plan: the services kept (an allowlist of keys; the rest left out or refused with the key's name), the main service (the first that publishes a port, else one with `tty` or `stdin_open`, else one built here, else the first), named volumes, and notes for the terminal. `composeModel()` makes the model compose runs: a forwarder service of the pinned Node image owning the main service's network namespace, every service with `cap_drop: ALL` plus `SERVICE_CAPS`, `SERVICE_LIMITS`, the owner and session labels and a bounded `local` log, one internal network when there are several, every `$` escaped. `previewContainer.ts` makes Relay's builder once per engine (`builder()`: `docker buildx create --driver docker-container`, its endpoint checked against the pinned one) and builds each service's image there from a tar of its snapshot folder (`docker buildx build --builder … --load -`), starts the forwarder and runs `LIMITS` in it, creates every container (`up --no-start`), copies bind-mounted project files into each created container (`copyMounts()`: `docker cp -` with a tar at their targets, which runs nothing; the main one's before it starts and after each recreation), attaches to the main one through the Engine API (`previewEngine.ts`, `agent: false`, the main container's ID checked), starts them (`up -d`), and waits for the port in the forwarder (`WAIT_PORT`, 30 seconds a time with `exec -i` and its stdin held open) and for the exit with `docker wait`. A Run again that cannot start reports `exited` with code -1 and keeps the services. Relay's builder is made once per engine even when previews start together (`creatingBuilders`), an image the engine has is not pulled, and a main service whose image serves a database (`isBacking()`) is not watched for a page. Page requests use `previewForwarder()` with the port as the forwarder's argument. **Run again** recreates the main container only (`up --no-start --no-deps --force-recreate`, attach, `docker start`). Everything a session made carries `io.harness-relay.preview.session`, which `sweepPreviewResources()` removes on failure and stop (containers with `-v`, one sweep at a time, a resource another sweep removed meanwhile taken as removed), as startup does with the owner label. The terminal stream holds at most 2 MB for a browser that stops reading, then ends, and the browser reconnects to the replay.

Build mode runs `npm run build`, extracts the public output including `dist/index.html` (the member's own `dist` for a workspace member) and removes the container. Server mode runs `npm start` and waits up to 30 seconds for `127.0.0.1:4173`. `previewDockerWire.ts` forwards HTTP requests to that fixed port through Docker exec. To exercise a real engine, run `npx tsx scripts/preview-runtime-check.ts` for React/Vite, SQLite create, read, update, delete and restart, and a workspace member's backend. Linux CI also runs that standalone check. To exercise the signed-in panel with a real frontend build and SQLite backend, and container previews (a Python `Dockerfile` app, a terminal app with **Run again**, **Open a shell** and its exit code, and a compose app with Redis and a mounted file), set `E2E_PREVIEW_DOCKER=1` and run `npx playwright test e2e/specs/app-preview-docker.spec.ts`. Linux end-to-end CI enables these scenarios. Locally the spec gives its server your Docker folder (`DOCKER_CONFIG`, else `~/.docker`), where Docker Desktop and OrbStack keep the Compose plugin. The regular unit suite runs offline.

### App publishing

`src/server/publish/` publishes a running preview to the user's own Render and Neon accounts ([architecture](architecture.md#publishing-an-app), [proposal](proposals/app-publish.md)). `plan.ts` is pure: `publishSpec()` turns a preview's mode, entry and files into Render's service type and details, the variables and the databases (a compose file through the preview's own `previewPlan()`), refusing with the reason what Render's free plan cannot run as one service; `databaseEnv()` puts Neon's values in place of the compose file's, then the user's over them. `render.ts` and `neon.ts` are the two APIs over `http.ts`'s `hostJson()` (bounded, no redirect, the host's message in errors); `git.ts` makes the repository through gh and pushes a `git fast-import` commit; `initdb.ts` runs the init scripts with `psql` in a container of Relay's own; `store.ts` keeps the keys and records; `index.ts`'s `AppPublisher` runs each publish as a job of steps. `AppPreviews.publishSource()` hands it the preview's files again, refused when their digest changed. To try it against stand-ins, set `RELAY_RENDER_API_URL`, `RELAY_NEON_API_URL` and `RELAY_PUBLISH_GIT_DIR` (`e2e/support/cloud.ts` is one).
## Adding a new agent CLI

The order of work around these steps (installing the CLI apart, what to find out about it first, recording, checking on each system, the docs) is in [Adding a coding agent](adding-a-coding-agent.md).

There are two routes. A CLI with a sign-in of its own (like Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code) gets a **native adapter**: below full access it runs in the CLI's own OS sandbox, or, with none (as Cursor and Antigravity), asks about every command (a Cursor team's own auto-run allowlist aside, which Harness Relay cannot clear). An open-source CLI that runs on the model backends gets an **open agent** adapter, almost entirely in its own file, on the generic ACP turn when it speaks ACP.

### A native adapter

1. **Types** — add the id to `Provider`, `PROVIDERS` and `PROVIDER_LABEL` in `src/shared/types.ts`, and what its runs can leave out of its own setup (`NATIVE_OFF_SUPPORT`, `NATIVE_ITEM_SUPPORT`; empty when it has no switch).
2. **Adapter** — create `src/server/agents/<name>.ts`:
   - a `create<Name>Parser(emit)` (or update handler) that turns the CLI's output into `AgentEvent`s (`session`, `text`, `text_end`, `thinking`, `plan`, `tool_start` with the call's `kind` when the CLI says what it does or the adapter can tell, `tool_end` with `output` / `error`, and `detail` when the CLI only says what a call was about once it is done) and remembers the final result/error;
   - an `AgentRunner` whose `run()` builds the arguments (prompt, system prompt, session new/resume, permission, model, effort), calls `runProcess()` and throws `AgentRunError` on failure (`aborted = true` when stopped);
   - approvals: when the CLI asks to do something the permission does not cover, call `o.requestApproval({ kind, detail, reason, tool })` and answer the CLI with the result (when `requestApproval` is absent, refuse).
   Keep the parser pure so it can be tested with recorded output lines.
3. **Register** it in `src/server/agents/index.ts`.
4. **Settings** — default binary name in `DEFAULT_BIN`, the variable that moves its home (`AGENT_HOME_ENV`), the variables it takes a sign-in from (`CREDENTIAL_ENV`) and, if the CLI reads an API key from the environment, `API_KEY_ENV` (`src/server/settings.ts`); a CLI with a sign-in also gets its keys in `OWN_KEY_VARS` (`src/server/agentEnv.ts`), and each of them in `MODEL_KEY_VARS`, or the server's environment never gives them to it (`test/agent-env.test.ts` checks the lists).
5. **Status & login** — `checkAuth()` and `LOGIN_METHODS` in `src/server/providers.ts`; the logout command in `logout()`; how much of an account's plan is used, when the CLI can say so without a prompt, in `askUsage()` (`src/server/usage.ts`, with the variables of a key its provider bills by use in `BILLED_KEY_ENV`), and what Settings says when it cannot (`usage.notReported` in both locale files).
6. **Models** — a discovery function in `src/server/models.ts` (or a static list). A CLI that can also run on the user's own endpoints of Settings → Model connections (as Claude Code, Codex, Grok and Copilot can) goes in `EndpointCli` and `isEndpointCli()`, with the API it calls and the efforts its models take there in `ENDPOINT_CLI` (`src/shared/types.ts`), and its adapter takes a member's endpoint model with `endpointRunModel()` (`src/server/backends.ts`), which fails the turn on a deleted endpoint's model.
7. **What it loads by itself** — a probe in `PROBES` (`src/server/native.ts`), its tabs in `src/web/native.ts`, and why it cannot leave out a part (`native.offNotes`) in both locale files.
8. **UI** — a color variable in `styles.css` (`--<name>`), `PROVIDER_COLOR` and `newAgent()` in `src/web/util.ts`, the key's variable and placeholder in `SettingsPage.tsx` (`KEY_ENV`, `KEY_PLACEHOLDER`), API-key help text in both locale files (`settings.apiKeyHelp`).
9. **Messages** — login method labels in `src/server/i18n.ts` (`login`), in both languages.
10. **Bug report form** — the CLI's name among the `agents` options of `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml` (`test/community.test.ts` checks them against `PROVIDER_LABEL`).

Most of these are `Record<Provider, …>`, so `npm run typecheck` names the ones you missed, among them how AgentsView reaches the CLI's sessions (`AGENTSVIEW_SESSIONS` in `src/server/agentsview.ts`: the prefix AgentsView names them with, or `recorded` when it cannot read them where the CLI keeps them, and Harness Relay sends its own record of each member's instead). It cannot name a few: the `provider` choices in the lead agent's prompts (`src/server/planner.ts`), the model family of a CLI's own models, for when it lists none of them (`CLI_FAMILY` in `src/server/roleGuides.ts`), and how its model ids name their family (`modelFamily()`, with a guide in `ROLE_GUIDES` for a family the lead has none for yet), the CLI's own folder in `agentFolders()` (`src/server/protect.ts`), whether its instruction files are the ones it is documented to read when it cannot be asked (`memoryFromFiles` in `failedProbe()`, `src/server/native.ts`), whether an account besides the default one can keep a sign-in of its own (`keyOnlyAccount()` in `src/shared/types.ts`), the variables of its sign-in that the open agents must not inherit (`INHERITED_BACKEND_VARS` in `src/server/backends.ts`), the project folders it loads hooks or MCP servers from, which the relay sandbox keeps read-only in edit mode (`PROJECT_AGENT_CONFIG` in `src/server/relaySandbox.ts`) and edit-mode writes ask about in the Antigravity and Muse Code adapters (`AGENT_CONFIG` in `src/server/agents/antigravity.ts`), and, for a run that gets a folder of its own in `data/tmp`, how that folder's name begins, so that a start removes those of turns the server was stopped during (`RUN_TMP_PREFIXES` in `src/server/protect.ts`).

The newest worked examples are Copilot and Muse Code. Copilot (`copilot.ts`): ACP in the CLI's own sandbox, whose policy each run checks before the prompt (the CLI's own answer to `/sandbox policy`), a home of its own for each run, the user's own endpoints too, and its tests replaying messages recorded from the real CLI (`test/fixtures/copilot/`, scrubbed) through a stand-in (`test/fake-copilot.ts`). Muse Code (`muse.ts`): ACP in the CLI's own sandbox, a config folder of its own written for each run (its permission profile can be chosen only in its settings file), the session's mode set on every turn, the briefing as its developer prompt, and its tests made the same way (`test/fixtures/muse/`, `test/fake-muse.ts`). Antigravity (`antigravity.ts`) is the worked example of one without a sandbox: ACP, settings of its own written for each run, the briefing in its system prompt, and its tests made the same way (`test/fixtures/antigravity/`, `test/fake-antigravity.ts`).

### An open agent

An **open agent** runs on the model backends, with no sign-in, in a home of Harness Relay's own and, below full access, in the relay sandbox. Read [Open agents](open-agents.md) first: what the five share, and one section per agent, each a worked example.

1. **Types** — its id in `OpenProvider` / `OPEN_PROVIDERS` and `PROVIDER_LABEL` in `src/shared/types.ts`, and an entry in each `Record<Provider, …>` (the typechecker lists them: `DEFAULT_BIN`, `PROVIDER_COLOR`, the locales' notes, `NATIVE_OFF_SUPPORT` / `NATIVE_ITEM_SUPPORT`, the native panel's tabs…).
2. **Adapter** — `src/server/agents/<name>.ts` exports an `OpenAgentDefinition`, registered in `openAgents` (`index.ts`). Its `process` (`OpenAgentProcess`) declares its home: `home(root, state)` gives the variables that point the CLI there, its `configPaths` in the shared home, and its `statePaths`, everything a run writes, inside `state`, the conversation member's own folder (`<home>/private/<key>`; a sandboxed run does not start otherwise), with caches that load code in the run's temp folder or off; `prepare` (writes the CLI's config before each run: provider, model, permission rules, briefing; keys only in variables, never in a file), `proxyEnv` and `emptyEnv`, and `sandbox` for any opening it needs (each one documented in its section of `docs/open-agents.md` and in `docs/sandboxing.md`). Leave `implemented: false` until it is checked against the real CLI: until then its runs fail at once.
   - **ACP**: fill in an `AcpAgentSpec` and use `acpRunner()`; every difference from the generic turn is one of its hooks (mode, model, effort, briefing, `describeCall`, `rejection` / `afterRefusal`, `filterUpdate`…). Configure the CLI to ask, so that `session/request_permission` reaches Harness Relay's decision.
   - **Any other protocol**: use `startOpenProcess()` (it reads the backend, makes the home and the run's temp folder, runs `prepare` and wraps the command in the relay sandbox) and speak the protocol yourself, sending every action through `acpPermissionDecision()` → `requestApproval` as the ACP agents do (see `pi.ts`).
3. **Models** — the definition's `efforts`, and `models` when the CLI has a catalog (only the configured backends' models are kept, as `<backend>/<model>`; each endpoint's are added).
4. **What it loads by itself** — the definition's `native` hook, and what it can leave out in `NATIVE_OFF_SUPPORT` / `NATIVE_ITEM_SUPPORT`.
5. **UI** — a colour (`--<name>` in `styles.css`, `PROVIDER_COLOR`) and its note on its Settings card (`settings.open.notes` in both locales).
6. **Tests and docs** — `test/<name>.test.ts` replays messages recorded from the real CLI (`test/fixtures/<name>/`, scrubbed) through a stand-in, and checks the configured permissions; add its section to `docs/open-agents.md` (both languages), with the version it was checked with.
7. **Bug report form** — the CLI's name among the `agents` options of `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml` (`test/community.test.ts` checks them against `PROVIDER_LABEL`).

## Checking a CLI's new version

The twelve CLIs update often, and an update can change what an adapter relies on without the offline tests noticing: they replay recordings of the versions the adapters were written against. `scripts/cli-check.ts` checks a new version against the real CLI in layers, cheapest first, and says what changed ([#103](https://github.com/Agentic-System-Lab/harness-relay/issues/103)). The version each CLI was checked with is in one place, `CHECKED_VERSIONS` in `src/shared/cliVersions.ts`: Settings installs the open agents' versions from it, the user guide lists the same (`test/cli-check.test.ts` holds them equal), and the shapes recorded with it are in `test/fixtures/cli-check/<cli>/`.

```bash
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts versions                 # 1: the version checked, the newest and the one here, for every CLI (--json: as JSON)
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts versions --issues --dry-run   # what the workflow would do with its issues, changing nothing
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts                          # each CLI here newer than the version it was checked with: layers 1 to 4, and 5's commands
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts claude --bin <path>      # one CLI, whatever its version (the new one, installed apart)
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts probe codex              # layer 2 alone
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts run opencode --only stop # layer 3 alone, the scenarios whose name holds the text
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts changelog copilot --to 1.0.113   # layer 4 alone (--from: another version than the one checked)
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts pi --bin <old> --record pi.json  # layers 2 and 3 of one version into a file, nothing compared
npx tsx scripts/cli-check.ts pi --bin <new> --against pi.json # another version compared with it, not with the shapes saved
```

An option it does not know is refused (`scripts/cli-check/args.ts`), and so are `--dry-run` without `versions --issues` and `--bin` without a CLI. A path given to `--bin` is taken from where you start it.

1. **Versions** (`scripts/cli-check/versions.ts`): the version checked, the newest its maker published, read where its own installer reads it (npm, PyPI, GitHub's releases, Grok's channel file, the version Cursor's install script downloads, Antigravity's manifest, Muse Code's channel: `VERSION_SOURCES`, every name encoded in its address), and what `--version` says here, in a scratch home. Only the numbers say which version is newer: another build of the same numbers (Cursor's hash after its date) counts as one to check. The **CLI versions** workflow ([`.github/workflows/cli-versions.yml`](../.github/workflows/cli-versions.yml)) runs this layer every day (`versions --issues`, `scripts/cli-check/issues.ts`) and does not open an issue just because a CLI is behind. An issue labelled `cli-check` is opened only when the check on Linux finds a failed check or something removed. When one is open and a newer version comes out, it updates the issue's title and body and says so in a comment; when the version checked moves forward but is still behind, it updates them without one; it closes the issue once the version checked has caught up with the one the issue told of (a source that names an older version again, as a CDN may while a release spreads, closes nothing). It then checks each new version on Linux once (see **On CI** below).
2. **Probes without a model** (`probe.ts`): status and sign-in, models, plan usage and what the CLI loads by itself, asked through the server's own code (`ProviderService`, `NativeSetupService`) as Settings asks them, and its `--help` and that of the subcommand its adapter starts (`HELP_ARGS`). Every process the server's code starts through `runProcess()` or `runShellScript()` is recorded (`recordSpawns()` in `src/server/process.ts`, a hook only this script sets, told nothing of the environment) and reduced to its shape (`shape.ts`): the kinds of messages each side sends (a JSON-RPC method, an answer named after its request, an ACP update by its `sessionUpdate`, a Claude Code control request by its subtype), the keys each holds (a map of names, such as a run's own profile name, as one `*`), their value types, and the values of the fields that say what a message is (`type`, `kind`, `stopReason`, `name`…), never text; and the help's flags, and what the server made of the answers. A few seconds a CLI, no model, no tokens. A CLI that says it is signed in stops the check (`SignedInError` in `layers.ts`): the scratch home holds no sign-in, so it found one elsewhere (the system's keychain), and nothing may run on that account.
3. **The real CLI on a scripted model** (`scenarios.ts`, `model.ts`): the scenarios of [Adding a coding agent, step 6](adding-a-coding-agent.md#6-record-it-and-test-it), each a turn through the adapter's own runner against the real CLI, recorded as in 2, on a model of the script's own on 127.0.0.1 that speaks Chat Completions, the Responses API (with its namespaces), the Anthropic Messages API and the Gemini API (and serves Muse Code's model catalog), streamed or not, and answers from a script: the scenario its prompt names has it call the shell tool the CLI offers (found by its name and parameters, its arguments filled from its schema), then reply. Its side being fixed, whatever changes between two versions' recordings is the CLI's. The scenarios and what each checks:
   - `reply`: the reply reaches the chat;
   - `command`: an `echo` runs in edit mode and its output goes back to the model;
   - `read-only-write`: a write in read-only leaves no file (a CLI that offers tools but no shell tool there, as Pi's run, passes; a turn that reached no model does not);
   - `outside-write`: in edit mode, a write outside the working directory leaves no file;
   - `protected-read`: the stand-in database's contents never reach the model;
   - `stop`: stopping a turn during a command that never ends ends it, and the command;
   - `guard-write`, `guard-write-full`: with the team's protected path `.env` given (`RunOptions.guard`), a command's write of it, in edit mode and with full access, leaves no file where the adapter says its CLI refuses it (`guardsShell()`), and only warns elsewhere;
   - `guard-tool-write`: the same with the CLI's own write tool (`writeCall()` in `model.ts` finds it by its name and parameters; `guardsTool()`);
   - `hook-before`, `hook-before-full`: with a team hook before commands that prints a mark and fails (`RunOptions.hooks`, run without Harness Relay's sandbox, with the check's own gateway for a CLI that runs the hook program), a command's write, in edit mode and with full access, leaves no file and the mark reaches the model, for a CLI in `HOOK_PROVIDERS` (only a warning for the others);
   - `hook-after`: with a team hook after commands that prints a mark and fails, the command runs and both its output and the mark reach the model;
   - `toolless`: only for the CLIs RLM's sub-model can be asked through (`SUB_MODEL_CLIS`), a turn with `RunOptions.toolless`, in the folder a question runs in (`questionDir()`), whose requests offer the model no tool at all and whose reply reaches the chat with nothing of the CLI's own before it; it saves no shape (`noShape`), having come after the shapes were saved;
   - `resume`: a second turn resumes the first one's session, which the model then holds.

   Every approval is answered: allowed for `command` and `stop`, refused for the others. Where a CLI asked before a refused command (as Cursor and Antigravity ask about every one), the check says so with a `!`: the refusal was the check's, and what keeps the CLI in once a command is allowed is not tried. Claude Code, Codex, Copilot and the open agents run on an endpoint, as on a model backend of the user's; Antigravity on the Gemini API's address with a key of its own, Muse Code on the Meta API's (its settings' `endpoint_transport`); Cursor (it runs only on its own service) and Grok (it needs its own sign-in even on an endpoint) have no layer 3 (`NO_RUN`). It also names a flag the adapter passes that the version checked listed in its help and this one no longer does. Every request the model got is kept in the scratch folder (`model-requests.jsonl`, with `--keep`).
4. **Release notes and the slower checks** (`changelog.ts`): the maker's notes between the version checked and the new one (`NOTES_SOURCES`: Claude Code's and Pi's CHANGELOG.md, GitHub's releases for Codex, Copilot, OpenCode, Goose, Cline and OpenHands, `agy changelog`; Grok, Cursor and Muse Code publish none it can read), their lines about permissions, sandboxes, hooks, trust, flags and environment variables, and anything removed, renamed or breaking first, then those about settings, sessions and sign-in: what no recording shows, such as a new setting that runs something without asking. And the slower checks the CLI needs: `scripts/sandbox-check.ts` for an open agent, `scripts/mcp-headers-check.ts` for a CLI in `GATEWAY_OVER_HTTP`.
5. **The live tests**, on a real model: the report names their commands for the CLI, only those that have tests for it (`E2E_LIVE_CLIS=<cli>`; see [Live tests](#live-tests)).

**Where it runs.** Layers 2 and 3 on macOS and Linux; 1 and 4 anywhere. Every CLI runs in scratch folders of its own (`scratch.ts`), made the script's whole environment before it imports any of the server's modules (they read the data folder as they load), and the script works in them: a home, the XDG folders, a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and Harness Relay's data folder of their own, with stand-ins of its database and key; of your variables only the `PATH`, the locale and the terminal's and user's names (`KEPT`), so no sign-in, key or config folder of yours is found; the keyring off, the CLIs' auto-updates off, no browser opened (agy is told it is over SSH; Cursor would then refuse to start). Harness Relay's data folder and the temporary folder are in `/tmp` (`CLI_CHECK_DIR` names another place), since its own sandbox needs a short path for its sockets. The home, the working directories and the folders outside them are in `/var/tmp/harness-relay-cli-check` (`CLI_CHECK_WORK_DIR`), outside the temporary folders, which the CLIs' sandboxes let commands write in (a write the check expects refused would go through there), and outside your home: the CLIs look for skills, instructions and settings in every folder above their working directory, and would load yours (the script refuses a `CLI_CHECK_WORK_DIR` inside it). Each working directory is a git repository, with `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` at the scratch folder. Both folders are removed at the end, also on Ctrl-C (with the CLIs it started), unless `--keep`. Nothing is signed in: what a real sign-in changes (the CLI's own models, its plan's usage) is for the live tests.

**On CI.** After keeping the issues, the CLI versions workflow checks each new version it has not checked yet, and skips one whose open issue already holds that version's report. A checked version is remembered, so a clean one is not run again. The memory is the previous successful run that was not a pull request: a pull request posts nothing and uploads no memory, so its success is not that run. A source that names an older version than the one remembered, or than an open issue tells of, is left alone: that version is not checked, the issue is not closed, and the remembered version stays (`scripts/cli-check/ci.ts`, one job per CLI on GitHub's Ubuntu runner, with Harness Relay's sandbox set up as the end-to-end tests set it up, unprivileged user and network namespaces allowed for the sandboxes Copilot and Muse Code bring (Ubuntu restricts them by default; [OS sandboxes by platform](sandboxing.md#linux) says what they need), slirp4netns and uv):
- it gets the new version and the version checked apart from where their makers publish them (`scripts/cli-check/obtain.ts`: npm, uv, GitHub's releases, the downloads the makers' own installers fetch, each checked against its maker's checksum where one is published, never an installer script; Muse Code's version checked by its full build, `MUSE_BUILDS`);
- records what the version checked says in layers 2 and 3 there (`--record`), then runs layers 1 to 4 for the new one compared with that (`--against`), not with the shapes saved, which were recorded on a Mac and would show the systems' differences too. Antigravity's version checked cannot be had (its manifest names only the newest), so it is compared with the shapes saved, and the report says so. A CLI Harness Relay runs only with full access on Linux (OpenCode: its adapter's `sandboxUnsupported`) runs its scenarios with full access there, and those below it are not run;
- opens an issue (label `cli-check`) only when the check found a failed check or something removed, and posts the report there, once for each version (a marker in the comment). A check that found neither opens none, and closes an open issue for that CLI. Before the folded report, the comment says what layers 2 and 3 found: nothing new, only new fields, what was removed, or a failed check, with those lines in a code block of their own. The whole report stays in a code block, where an `@name` from the release notes notifies nobody.

The checks run what they download, so their jobs get a token that reads only and no secret, and every CLI runs in scratch folders with none of the job's variables; only the jobs that keep the issues and post the reports can write issues, and neither runs a CLI. A pull request that changes the check (its scripts, `cliVersions.ts` or the workflow) runs the checks for every CLI's newest version, with a token that reads only, and posts nothing: each job's summary shows its report. `npx tsx scripts/cli-check/ci.ts check <cli> <version>` runs one check on this computer as the workflow does (in `$RUNNER_TEMP`, else the temporary folder).

**What to do with a report.** The comment says what layers 2 and 3 found before the folded report: nothing new, only new fields, what was removed, or a failed check. Open the report when it lists a removal or a failed check; the release notes stay folded. Each layer that found something says what: a check that failed (a `SECURITY:` line is a widening of what runs without asking), a kind of message gone or new, a key gone or its type changed, a flag the help no longer lists. Where layers 2 and 3 found nothing and the release notes' first lines say nothing about how the CLI runs, run its live tests, then move its version forward in `src/shared/cliVersions.ts`, in one commit with the new version's shapes (`--accept`, which saves a run only when all its scenarios passed) and the docs that name the version checked: the user guide's list, in both languages (held equal by a test), and, once they were tried again with it, where the docs say which version a behaviour was seen with (each agent's section in `docs/open-agents.md`, `docs/sandboxing.md`'s "What was tested", the landing page's versions tested in `site/`). The issue the workflow opened, when the check found a removal or a failed check, is closed from that pull request. A check that found neither opens no issue. Where something changed, fix the adapter first, with its recorded fixtures (`test/fixtures/<cli>/`), its stand-in (`test/fake-<cli>.ts`) and its end-to-end fake (`e2e/fake-cli/agents/<cli>.cjs`), as [Adding a coding agent](adding-a-coding-agent.md) does for a new CLI. A shape saved for one version can differ from the next run's on what the CLI says only sometimes (a question it answers late): run it again before reading much into one line. The default command checks only the CLIs here whose version is newer than the one checked; name a CLI to check whatever version it has. Antigravity has no saved shapes (`NO_SHAPES` in `layers.ts`: its version checked, 1.5.6, can no longer be downloaded), so its first check of a new version reports them all as new, and saves them with `--accept` once it passed. A new CLI gets its version checked and its shapes saved once it is checked, and the lists the script keeps per CLI: `VERSION_SOURCES`, `NOTES_SOURCES`, `HELP_ARGS` (in `probe.ts`), `placement()` in `scenarios.ts` or `NO_RUN`, and where its versions are, `artifactFor()` in `obtain.ts`. Moving Muse Code forward adds the new version's full build to `MUSE_BUILDS` (`muse --version` prints it).

## Database changes

The schema lives in the `MIGRATIONS` array in `src/server/db.ts`; `PRAGMA user_version` records how many have been applied. To change the schema:

1. **Append** a new SQL string to `MIGRATIONS` (never edit a released one).
2. Update the row types and queries in `store.ts` / `settings.ts`.
3. Add or update tests in `test/store.test.ts`.

Each migration runs in its own transaction at startup. [Architecture → Persistence](architecture.md#persistence) lists each table, with the schema version its later columns came in (41: long-term memory's notes; 40: a message Relay sent by itself to fix the team checks, `messages.auto_fix`; 39: what each CLI account's runs turn on, `native_on`, with the columns the agent CLIs' own features keep; 38: automation attachments, 37: task submission receipts; 36: team checks, `conversations.checks` and `presets.checks`, the list, `messages.checks`, a result, and `check_baselines`, where the run of a conversation's latest result started, which only the server reads). node:sqlite cannot bind `undefined`; use `nul()` for optional values, and `transaction(db, fn)` for multi-statement writes (it doesn't nest).

To inspect data:

```bash
sqlite3 data/relay.db '.tables'
sqlite3 data/relay.db 'select seq, author_kind, agent_name, status from messages order by conversation_id, seq'
```

## Changing prompts

- `buildBriefing()` — the system-level briefing (role, team, mode rules, workspace). Rebuilt every turn. `scheduleRules()` explains messages a schedule sent (`via="schedule"`), and with `unattended` (the run refuses approvals) says so.
- `buildTurnPrompt()` — the per-turn message with `<chat>` and the cue line.
- `buildSteerPrompt()` — a message the user sends into a member's running turn (steering), framed so the member answers it in that turn.
- `subagentRules()` / `SUBAGENT_WAIT_RULE` — the briefing's rule asking Antigravity, Grok and Muse Code to wait for their own subagents before ending a reply.
- `PLAN_SCHEMA`, `REVISION_SCHEMA` (`planner.ts`), `REVIEW_SCHEMA` (`reviewer.ts`), `GROUP_SCHEMA` (`grouping.ts`) — the JSON shapes the lead agent's turns ask Claude Code and Codex for (strict: every property required, null for none); change them with the prompts and the parsers they mirror.
- `forwardText()` — how a reply is shown to other agents (text + `<actions>` log).
- `parseMentions()` — mention parsing; text may follow a name directly, and code spans and fenced blocks of any kind (also unclosed ones) are ignored.
- `buildPlannerPrompt()` / `PLANNER_BRIEFING` (`planner.ts`) — what the lead agent is asked in auto-team mode, and the JSON it must return. For a schedule's message (`schedule`) it says the team gets it on a schedule, unattended, and asks for members named after the names it @mentions.
- `buildRevisePrompt()` / `REVISER_BRIEFING` (`planner.ts`) — what the lead agent is asked when the user wants the team in the team drawer changed: the request, the team, the conversation so far, a waiting design (for a question or the schedule), the schedule's message; it must return the whole team, naming a renamed or moved member as `was` (never a new member) and giving a role only for a new member or one it rewrites. It also says when the schedule is off or was deleted, and which saved folder the lead works in when the drawer names another one.
- `ROLE_GUIDES` / `roleGuidesForLead()` (`roleGuides.ts`) — how to write a role for each model family, which both prompts above give the lead agent where it writes roles (after `ROLE_RULE`, the rule for every role: its first line says who the member is, which is all teammates see of it, `roleSummary()`): only the guides of the families the signed-in CLIs can run (`familiesForLead()`: every model each one lists, as its new members' account runs it, and its default; for a CLI that lists none of its own models, its own family, `unlistedFamily()`), for the conversation's permission (read-only: no asking for edits), and none when no family is known. With guides, the lead's list of each CLI's models marks each model with its guide (`guide: Claude`, `guideMark()`, from `familyOf()`), and a model of no family (Cursor's Auto) with none. `modelFamily()` reads a model's family from its name (without the API or endpoint it is on); a model on the user's own endpoint is open weights, unless it is Claude, GPT, Gemini or Grok through a gateway, and an open agent's model through a model API whose name says nothing is that API's (OpenAI's GPT, Anthropic's Claude, xAI's Grok). Each guide holds only what changes how a role is written for that family, not what holds for every model: keep them short. They were surveyed on **2026-11-14** from each family's own guides: look there first when a family changes, and write the new date here. The sources, and how each family's stood then:
  - **Claude**: [prompting best practices](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/claude-prompting-best-practices) with a page for each current model next to it (Fable 5.2, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5), and [Claude Code best practices](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/best-practices). All current.
  - **GPT and Codex**: [prompt guidance](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-guidance), with a page for each model up to the GPT-6 family that Codex offers now, the [Codex prompting guide](https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/gpt-5/codex_prompting_guide) and the [GPT-5 prompting guide](https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/gpt-5/gpt-5_prompting_guide). The older GPT-5-Codex prompting guide's address answers 404, so its "less is more" advice was read from search results; no -codex model came after gpt-5.3-codex.
  - **Gemini**: [prompting strategies](https://aistudio.google.com/docs/prompting-strategies) (Gemini 3.x) and Antigravity's [subagents](https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents). Gemini 4 has no guide that could be read (its developer guide shows only with JavaScript), so Gemini 3.x's advice stands for it; Google took the [Vertex AI Gemini 3 prompting guide](http://web.archive.org/web/20260515024402/https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/start/gemini-3-prompting-guide) down (archived copy).
  - **Grok**: xAI's [best practices](https://docs.x.ai/developers/model-capabilities/text/best-practices) (Grok 4.5 to 4.7) and [project rules](https://docs.x.ai/build/features/project-rules). There is no prompting guide for Grok 4.x; the [grok-code-fast-1 prompt guide](http://web.archive.org/web/20260328045810/https://docs.x.ai/developers/advanced-api-usage/grok-code-prompt-engineering) went when that model was retired on 2026-05-15 (archived copy of March 2026).
  - **Muse**: no prompting guide for the hosted Muse models Muse Code runs (Meta's guides cover only its open-weight Muse models); from Meta's [Build with Muse Spark](https://dev.meta.ai/resources/blog/build-with-muse-spark/) and [multi-agent orchestration](https://dev.meta.ai/docs/cookbook/multi-agent-orchestration).
  - **Composer**: no prompting guide of its own; from Cursor's [agent best practices](https://cursor.com/blog/agent-best-practices) and [subagents](https://cursor.com/docs/subagents).
  - **Open-weight models**: no one guide; from the models' own cards, Goose's [benchmark of local models](https://goose-docs.ai/blog/2025/03/31/goose-benchmark) and OpenHands' [local LLMs](https://docs.openhands.dev/openhands/usage/llms/local-llms).

  Copilot runs models of these families, so its members follow their guides.
- `buildGroupPrompt()` / `GROUPER_BRIEFING` (`grouping.ts`) — how the lead agent is asked to sort a new conversation into the user's groups, and to name it (`name`: a title in the message's language, at most `TITLE_MAX` characters), in one run; `groupSchema()` asks for what it asks.
