- `test/checkCommands.test.ts` — team checks' commands: their environment (none of the server's secrets, `CI=1`, caches in the run's folder), what the sandbox refused read from the output (local ports and sockets by system, a read-only folder, Linux's entries at the top of a working directory, a browser, the network), the end of the output kept, where a command runs, how one went (passed, failed, could not run: a refusal, exit 127, a time limit, Stop; a test's own words about a host or a permission not taken for the sandbox's), the settings checks read (as full paths, the files a command line names from its folder), and running one (with full access in a shell of its own; below full access through the sandbox, with the fixed script, the spec and each opening on its own system only; when the sandbox cannot start or stopped it, on Windows, outside the working directories); a host the sandbox refused naming the network as the reason only with a network error in the output (else a failure with the hosts said), and Python's, Go's and Rust's words for a refused file call; the dependencies a check tree is given (each node_modules as links to its packages, caches left out; a venv; only in folders the tree has, never over its own, never in the CLIs' homes); a workspace's own packages linked to the tree, and a stopped server's trees removed without following their links.
- `test/writeGuard.test.ts` — the team's protected paths for a CLI to refuse writing (`src/server/writeGuard.ts`): made only when the list protects something, its patterns tidied; a protected path in any working directory, also one not made yet and one reached through a link; as absolute globs (anchored, at any depth, what is under each); what matches now (`.git` and `node_modules` left out, within limits); as sandbox-runtime's globs (a folder's own wildcards as sets of one, `**` in a name as `*`, no negated set, none too long).
- `test/hooks.test.ts` — the team's hooks (`src/server/hooks.ts`): a list with hooks alone has rules but nothing to check after the fact, and which calls a hook covers; the scripts a hook's command runs as protected paths (a command's program named by a path, an interpreter's script, quoted names; never its other arguments, a program on the PATH, a full path, one that leaves the folder or one only the shell knows); what a hook is given (the call in its environment, never its command line; long arguments cut), a call named for the user, a program's words quoted for a shell; running a turn's hooks for real (the first before hook that fails refuses the call and the rest are not run; only the hooks of the call's kind and moment; a time limit, a folder outside the working directories and Windows refusing it; a call too long to read refused without running a hook; the hooks' own deadline stopping them and refusing; after hooks' failures told to the agent and noted; a check command's environment; at most a few notes; the kinds and the time each moment needs); the answers for a CLI that takes Claude Code's; the hook program through a real gateway socket (each call passed to its turn, its answer and exit code printed; a refusal of its own, exit 2, without an answer, a token gone with its turn, no answer within its time limit, or a call too large before it runs, which it never cuts; none without a listening gateway or Node.js; the grants a turn took ended with it).
- `test/markdown.test.ts` — mention chips follow the server's rule and skip code and links (also Chinese right after a bare URL); a bare URL written right against Chinese text ends where the Chinese starts; images are shown as links; local images and videos are shown through the app; links to local files (also `file:` URLs, reference links and files written as images) become downloads through the app (a line number or anchor dropped, never another computer's share), while links to sites, anchors and folders stay as they are; reference images of local media are shown; a link is checked with HEAD first (found, missing, or unknown when the server cannot say), and a missing file keeps the link and its text with the reason after it; the check waits a moment and is dropped for a URL that changed; a click on a missing file checks again and clicks once more when it is there, without a loop; the user's own messages keep their line breaks; table cells stay on one line or wrap at a readable width, so wide tables scroll sideways.
- `test/orchestrator-harness.ts` — not a test file: what the orchestrator's tests below share. A fake runner swapped into `runners` (a fake claude CLI for the members, the lead agent (designing, changing, reviewing and tidying the long-term memory) and the group sorter, steered by `fake`), a new database, store, orchestrator and project folder before each test, and a time limit of 20 s a test (`slow()`, so longer on Windows), as each one runs git several times. The tests are split by topic into the files below, which Vitest runs side by side (the tests of one file run one after another).
- `test/orchestrator.test.ts` — full runs with the fake runner: requests that arrive while a run is set up or a result is applied, agents asking the user (the team stops; the one that asked answers first after the reply, in roundtable, parallel and relay; in relay the teammates its reply handed to speak after it, Continue handing to them too, and the handoffs counted afresh once the user answered; Continue skips it; a reply to someone else, or an edit, starts afresh; messages written meanwhile wait for the answer), messages sent while the team replies (delivered before the next turn of an agent they are for, in the order sent; a follow-up run for what no turn took; kept after Stop; sent first by an interrupt, the next message or Continue; withdrawn; kept across a restart), deleting a conversation mid-run, keeping a session when a fresh start fails too, and telling teammates what a failed turn changed. How many conversations have agents at work (`busy()`, what an update of the desktop app waits for): each replying one from the moment its run is set up, not one waiting for the answer to its question; the lead agent changing a team or sorting a conversation, a change being made to it, and a branch being made. Also passing what the user turned off of a CLI's own setup (all of a kind and single items) to every member's run, and to the lead agent's and the group sorter's. Also telling AgentsView after every turn and at the end of the run (with the agents' sessions known by then), linking the lead agent's and the group sorter's sessions (and sending again when the sorter ends after the run), telling it nothing about a conversation deleted while it replies, and a run kept whole when telling fails. Also accounts: each member runs in its account's home, a member moved to another account starts a fresh session, an account removed since counts as the default one (its session goes on); the lead agent runs as its account, and the members it designs get a signed-in account; each member runs with its account's default model and effort (its own first) and leaves out what its account leaves out, and the lead agent, the group sorter and a team revision with the lead's account's. Also approvals with a fake agent (asking and waiting, "always allow", a line kept as one exact command whose rule allows no program in it by name, a decline note, expiry on stop, refusing automatically), retrying a reply, editing a user message (`resend`: later messages are dropped and every agent starts a fresh session), attached files (saved with safe names, images and paths delivered to each agent, kept when a message is edited, deleted with dropped messages, files-only messages, bad uploads), and a tool row taking the detail a CLI only gives when the call is done. Also a turn in an open agent's home other than its latest run's (the agent-home folder changed, or back in an earlier one) starting fresh with that folder emptied, and a deleted conversation's state removed from every home it has been in. Also a member whose model moves between the CLI's own service and an endpoint, or to another endpoint, starting a fresh session with the whole history, while another model of the same backend resumes it (the backend is recorded with the session). Also every member's reply added in the team's order in parallel mode, whichever CLI each runs on. Also each member's state key (the same at every turn, another one in another conversation, the lead agent's and the group sorter's own), a permission that went up starting a fresh session with the whole history and an open agent's state folder emptied by the orchestrator (recorded per CLI in the database: back on a CLI whose last run was read-only empties its folder; a folder that cannot be emptied stops the turn and is emptied at the next one; a session never recorded counts as read-only; Claude Code only starts afresh) while one that went down resumes, a member's state removed with its conversation, a request after its turn ended refused without a card or a lock, and an open agent without a model running on its picker's default (Settings' first, a member's own before that). Also a subagent's calls kept under the call that started it, the agent's words around them kept in one text part, a call said to be a subagent's only by a second start put under it then, and a CLI's note kept in the reply.
- `test/acp-turn.test.ts` — the generic ACP turn against a fake ACP agent (`test/fake-acp.ts`): updates → `AgentEvent`s (text, thinking, plan, tool calls with their kind (ACP's, or the spec's `toolKind`) and their output, usage and cost; the cached tokens and the reasoning counted in the input and output as the answer's total says the CLI counted them, `acpTokens()`), the model by `session/set_model` or a config option and the effort, only when they differ, or not at all when the config sets them, the session mode for the permission, a refused value; the backend's keys and the home reaching the process, extra session fields; `session/resume` or `session/load` as advertised (the replayed history not shown), and a failed or impossible resume failing before anything is said; a permission request asked, allowed, declined with a note handed to the model, or refused automatically, edit asking only about pushing, the briefing explaining refusals when the agent takes no reason, `describeCall` seeing earlier and recent calls (a request that names no call), `afterRefusal` after the answer and `afterSession` before the prompt; steering through the spec's `steer` while the prompt runs (given with `onSteer`, taken back as the turn ends, none for an agent without the hook); requests for files and terminals never done (refused, or answered as the spec says); `filterUpdate`, `emptyEnv`, `exitGraceMs`; rows left open closed when the turn ends; stopping (`session/cancel`), a stop at any step before the prompt (the sandbox check, `prepare`, `afterInitialize`, `session/new`, `afterSession`, mode, model) ending the turn without sending it, a request the user was asked about answered `cancelled` when the turn is stopped meanwhile, a crash, the agent ending the turn itself, the timeout; an adapter not done or a model that cannot run refused before anything starts. Also the open agent process: the relay sandbox's spec (writes, read-only config, protected paths, the backend's host), each run's own temp folder (removed after), proxy variables only in the sandbox, failing closed when the sandbox cannot start and on native Windows. And the pieces: the permission decision by kind and command (an argument list read as its command line), the approval card (read-only stays a boundary; a request that names nothing gets no "always allow"), the options chosen (never an "always" one, by id when the spec says), MCP servers in ACP's form, usage, the prompt (briefing, images, a leading `/`), a to-do tool's plan. Also a turn its model refused failing with the rows it left open closed as not finished. A run with other working directories hands them to the sandbox (`extraCwds`). Also an HTTP server with a stdio stand-in (Harness Relay's gateway): given as HTTP to an agent that takes it, else its stand-in, made only then.
- `test/cli-install.test.ts` — installing a CLI from Settings (`src/server/cliInstall.ts`) with a fake computer (no network, no real installer): a recipe for every CLI, the open agents at the versions the user guide names, the command each shows, where each program is (npm's global layout, on Windows its `.cmd`; uv's link; the scripts' `bin`); npm started from the PATH (and Homebrew's on macOS), on Windows as the Node beside `npm.cmd` running npm's script, never the script; Node.js's build for each system and processor (its archive, its checksum, only Node and npm taken out of it; none for another system or processor, nor a Linux with musl), laid out in the folder as npm's global folder is beside its Node, offered for Claude Code, Codex and Copilot only, at the version the user guide names; without npm, its archive downloaded, checked and unpacked, that Node checked to run, then the CLI installed with its npm (that Node first on the PATH), Node and npm left in the folder and the download gone, and on Windows unpacked with System32's tar, never Git for Windows' GNU tar on the PATH; a download that does not match its checksum not used, and a Node that does not run there (on Linux, without libatomic) said so before npm runs, each in either language; what Node.js's build needs that is missing (curl, tar) and npm asked for where there is no build, and for the open agents; the installers' environment (no Claude Code nesting markers nor `npm start`'s own variables; a maker's script with the folder as its home and none of the variables that would move it out; uv's tools, Python and cache in the folder); npm's and uv's caches the install's own, in the folder, and gone once it ends; an installer's output without colours or redrawn progress bars; what is missing for each (npm, uv, curl, bash, or Windows for the scripts); an install with npm, a script (downloaded first, then run, then deleted) and uv, its note, its version check and the CLI's executable path set to it; uv's own installer into the folder first when uv is not on the PATH (`UV_UNMANAGED_INSTALL`), then OpenHands with that uv, and on Windows its PowerShell installer and OpenHands on the x64 Python; npm started with its own folder first on the PATH (Homebrew's on macOS); uv's installer given none of the variables that would send uv elsewhere (`UV_INSTALL_DIR`, `CARGO_DIST_FORCE_INSTALL_DIR`); on Windows the makers' PowerShell installers (as a script block, by fixed text with the script and its arguments in its environment, never `-File`; the folder as `USERPROFILE`, `LOCALAPPDATA` and `APPDATA`, the program's folder first on the `Path`) and Goose's bash script in Git for Windows' bash (its paths written `C:/…`); on Windows the user's PATH put back as it was before each script, after one that turned its `%USERPROFILE%` entries into the folder's, one that failed and one that was stopped, none run when it cannot be read or its answer is not one, tried again before the next script and at startup (`recover()`) when it could not be put back, one such script at a time, and the fixed scripts that read and put it back (no double quotes, what they work on in their environment); Git for Windows' bash found beside its `git` or where it installs, never WSL's, and Windows PowerShell in System32; what is missing on Windows (Git Bash for Goose, curl, PowerShell); the links an installer left in the PATH's folders that lead into the CLI's folder removed (also dangling ones), nothing else, and never those in the CLI's own folders (its `bin` on the PATH, also under another name); a failed install (its output kept, its words in either language), one that left no program or whose program does not run, cancelled (also during its version check), stopped with the server (which waits for them, and counts those under way), each removing its folder; installing again over what an earlier one left; a note taken only for its own CLI and a program inside its folder; removing (only Harness Relay's, the executable path set back to the one before unless the user set another meanwhile; refused while agents are at work or it installs; no install while it is being removed, nor a second removal); and the HTTP routes.
- `test/cli-install-web.test.ts` — the assistant overview’s setup steps, additional accounts and open agents’ backend status. A CLI's install on its card: what it shows (nothing, Install and Try again, why it cannot, under way, Harness Relay's with Remove and whether it is in use), a "not installed" checked before the install shown as still checking, the installs polled while one runs and those that ended named once, those under way followed once Settings is closed until they end (for at most longer than an install may take, an answer that does not come asked again), one answer put in place, the end of the installer's output.
- `test/sandbox-setup.test.ts` — what the agents' sandboxes need and installing it (`src/server/sandboxSetup.ts`) with a fake computer: on Linux the packages missing with each package manager's names (apt, dnf, pacman, zypper), whose sandboxes run them and the command, also programs on the PATH; no package manager, or no way to get administrator rights (sudo asking a password, no desktop session for pkexec), said so; root, `sudo -n` (asked once a minute at most) and pkexec in a desktop session; Ubuntu's restriction on user namespaces and the AppArmor profile asked for unless one lets `/usr/bin/bwrap` make them (Claude Code's docs' or Harness Relay's), nothing without the restriction or AppArmor; the desktop app's Node.js on macOS and Linux when none is on the PATH (none from the source, nor on Windows), first, and said when Node.js has no build for the processor; without curl or libatomic, those among the packages (each package manager's name for libatomic, looked for in the library folders of the processor) and Node.js after them (waiting for them by hand without administrator rights); the install in one run of the fixed script with sudo or pkexec (what it installs in its environment, through `env`) or as root, pkexec's dismissed prompt said in either language, another failure with its exit code, what is still missing afterwards named; the desktop app's Node.js put in its folder first, checked, and nothing of it left when its download does not check; without curl, the packages first, then Node.js with the curl they brought; an install that succeeds with what it can, leaving what it cannot listed; nothing to install, one at a time, cancelled and stopped with the server; the script itself (fixed text, package names without shell syntax) run against stand-ins of apt-get, which updates its lists only when a package cannot be found, and apparmor_parser; the HTTP routes.
- `test/git-install.test.ts` covers Git discovery and the platform installer choices with fake processes, including the Apple installer handoff, missing privileges, retries and Windows PATH discovery.
- `test/agent-env-web.test.ts` — the keys a CLI is not given, on the page (`src/web/agentEnv.ts`): the CLIs New chat's notice lists (in Settings' order, the installed ones only), shown once in this browser and without storage too; a name added to a pass-through list as the server takes it (trimmed, once, while there is room) and removed.
- `test/sandbox-setup-web.test.ts` — the sandboxes' setup on the page (`src/web/sandboxSetup.ts`): when New chat asks (something missing, no install running, this browser not asked yet; also when nothing can be installed here), when Install does anything, what to run by hand, whose sandboxes, and a browser with no storage.
- `test/cline.test.ts` — the Cline adapter: its home and the run's own `CLINE_DIR` (briefing rules file, global, MCP and provider settings, the feature-flag cache) for each backend kind × permission, its environment (local session backend, telemetry and flags off, only `CLINE_API_KEY`, its own variables emptied) and arguments (`--auto-approve`, `--thinking`), the relay sandbox's spec and opt-in; whole turns replayed by a stand-in (`test/fake-cline.ts`) from messages recorded with the real `cline --acp` (`test/fixtures/cline/`): text, reads, `apply_patch` and editor edits, commands, approvals allowed and declined, refused automatically, failed calls reported `completed`, web search and fetch, thinking, MCP from its settings file, full access, stopping (also while a permission request waits: answered `cancelled`), `session/load` (history dropped, usage from its session record), a resume that fails, a bad key, model or endpoint, a crash; its tool calls, outputs and errors; its catalog from `session/new` and what it loads by itself from `cline config <kind> --json`. Also its subagents left out (`spawn_agent` in `disabledTools`).
- `test/backends.test.ts` — model backends: `<backend>/<model>` ids; the store without a credential store, with one (`backend-<id>-api-key`, `endpoint-<id>-api-key`, never in the database), moving keys from the database into it and scrubbing them, a store that refuses or cannot be read, the endpoint limit; a run's backend (model, only its own backend's key, endpoint, hosts) and what it says when a model cannot run; an open agent's environment (the configured keys instead of inherited ones, its own home in the data folder or the agent-home folder); masks (the last four characters of a long key only) and Harness Relay's own address refused as an endpoint however it is spelled, also on the port `npm run dev`'s Vite took; the ports `npm run dev` set for itself never in a CLI's environment, `npm start`'s `PORT` kept; the HTTP API (masked keys, setting and removing them, endpoints and what is refused about them); an open agent's status (installed, its adapter done, a backend set up; no sign-in) and models (its catalog's for configured backends, the endpoints' saved models); the endpoint form's logic. Also each endpoint model's context window and output limit: kept after a restart, the user's over the server's for a run, only for the models listed, the server's dropped with another address and kept by a new name only, a model named `__proto__` a key like another, an output limit the server's window cannot hold refused with nothing changed, only figures Settings takes read back from the database; read over HTTP from vLLM's catalog (a LoRA adapter getting its base model's), llama.cpp's `meta.n_ctx` (never `n_ctx_train`), a model's own record, none from a server that reports none, and nothing asked for a new name only; what the API refuses; a run's limits and the shares kept for an answer and a tool output; and the form's figures as typed, where each shown comes from, and its problems. Also the model menus and the agents' status refreshed after a change that failed part way (an endpoint saved while the credential store refused its key). Claude Code, Codex and Grok on the endpoints: which models run on one (`endpointModel()`; their own ids, with a `/` too, stay theirs), the endpoint's key in its variable (one inherited taken out), the efforts sent there, the endpoints' models after each CLI's own in its menu, with the effort its "default" runs, and Claude Code's menu asked again after the endpoints change; their runs get the endpoints, never the API backends' keys. Also the endpoints deleted in Settings: remembered after a restart until one of the same id is added again, given to Claude Code's, Codex's and Grok's runs (not Cursor's), a model of one failing the run (`endpointRunModel()`) while any other model, one with a slash too, goes to the CLI as it is; Cursor left out (no endpoint models in its menu, no backends for its runs, and its menu kept when the endpoints change, while Claude Code's, Codex's and Grok's, every account's, are dropped); and an open agent's environment without `CURSOR_API_KEY`, which Cursor's keeps. Also the deleted endpoints' ids listed in the view and forgotten when asked (the route too: 404 for one not remembered, 400 for a bad id), so that a CLI's own model with the same start goes to it again, and a deleted endpoint still remembered when adding it again could not be saved. After such a failed save the endpoint is not kept in memory either, and adding it again then clears its id in memory and on disk. An add or an edit whose save fails keeps nothing of it, in memory or in the credential store: an added endpoint's key does not stay behind, an edit's endpoint and key are as they were. After a failed edit replaced a key the credential store could not give at startup, the endpoint shows no key, not an unreadable one (when the store will not take the new key out either, the warning says it still holds that key, not that the key is gone); an edit that left the key alone keeps it marked unreadable.
- `test/agent-env.test.ts` — which model keys of the server's environment each CLI is given (`src/server/agentEnv.ts`, `SettingsStore.env()`): every variable the open agents lose sorted into a key or an address, each CLI's own sign-in among the keys and kept its own, GitHub's and the cloud's tokens and the addresses never withheld; each CLI's default account with its own keys only and everything else of the server's environment; the open agents as before, whatever is passed on; the names the user passes on, to that CLI only, for each of its accounts (another still without the CLI's own sign-in variables), kept across a restart and withheld again once taken off; the keys Codex's `config.toml` names (tables, inline tables, comments skipped, a file too large or missing, none from one that is not a plain file), the account's own home's for each account; the one variable a default account signs in with, in each CLI's own order (Claude Code's cloud switches, `0` not counting, then `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` before `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; Copilot's `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN` before `GH_TOKEN`), none once a key Settings keeps comes first; the list kept through the CLI's other settings and as it was when it cannot be saved; a CLI's own sign-in variable passed on to its default account, never its other accounts; the pass-through list checked (trimmed, once, names only, at most `MAX_PASS_ENV`) as it is set and as it is read back; Settings' view with names only, and none for an open agent.
- `test/openhands.test.ts` — the OpenHands adapter: the run's own home and settings (a whole agent per backend kind and permission, no key in the file, subagents only with full access, the tool library in `mcp.json`, the terminal shell's `.bashrc`, tmux or a plain pseudo-terminal, the variables a project `.env` could set), cleaning up its tmux server, its approvals (the announced calls, a request about several asked about unless the permission covers all of them, a request with no call announced never allowed by itself, actions an earlier turn left rejected before a resume, subagents refused below full access, its own option ids), its updates (rows, thoughts and replies cleaned, results from observations, usage less the session's earlier count), its errors, turns replayed from the real CLI (`test/fixtures/openhands/`), its LiteLLM model catalog and what it loads by itself. Also a message that only looks like its notice of a failure held back and shown once anything comes after it or the prompt answers, in its place. Also its terminal's control sequences (bracketed paste, colours) taken out of a command's output. On Windows its home is its `USERPROFILE` too. Also the condenser's `max_tokens` from an endpoint model's context window (the window less an answer's share and the tool definitions'), the agent's `max_output_tokens` from its output limit, never `max_input_tokens`, and none of it without a window or on an API backend.
- `test/relaySandbox.test.ts` — Harness Relay's own sandbox, offline: what each permission may write, the protected and config paths, the opt-ins (a pty, the run's Unix sockets and trustd on macOS only, quietly denied hosts, connect-only ports), each run's own config and host temp folder readable inside the private roots, host names never looked up by the server (asked about like any other, a literal local address refused without asking), the failure message said once (packages named on Linux only), backend hosts (loopback and local destinations, a port required on this computer, never Harness Relay's), Harness Relay's own ports (`PORT`, the one the server got for 0, 8787, `npm run dev`'s 8789 and 5173, and the port its Vite took when 5173 was in use), refusals before a run (patterns, edit mode in Harness Relay or the home folder, writable folders that hold what decides how agents run), the environment (only loopback kept out of the proxy, or only the agent's own ports when a backend is on loopback, which the proxy lets through on macOS only), asking about a host through the right run's approvals and keeping two runs apart, run folders and their marker file, the startup sweep of what a killed server left running, the agent started through the fixed exec script with its arguments and variables in the environment (run for real), the host process and its control channel, the Node that runs the host (the server's, or in the desktop app one on `PATH`), and failing closed (native Windows, missing programs, a desktop app without a new enough Node.js on `PATH`, a processor without the seccomp helper). It never starts a real sandbox: `scripts/sandbox-check.ts` does. Also the other spellings of a local address (`127.1`, `2130706433`, `0x7f.0.0.1`, `0177.0.0.1`, a trailing dot, `0`, the metadata address as a number) read as the address the proxy dials, refused without asking, and a name asked about once whatever its case; the host's temp folder in the data folder's `srt/`, a data folder too long for its sockets refused (no folder in the system's temp folder); the startup sweep keeping everything, with no permission given back, while a sweep found a survivor or could not tell. Also the loopback ports a run may reach relayed onto the sandbox's own loopback through the proxy on Linux (`socat` started by the fixed exec script with the ports, its path and the run's proxy credentials, percent-decoded, from the environment), and taken from the run's hosts. Also that a project's `.cursor` folder stays read-only in edit mode, with the other agents' configuration (`PROJECT_AGENT_CONFIG`): Cursor, which has no sandbox, would read rules, subagents, skills or an MCP config planted there; Antigravity's `.agents` and its other spellings (`.agent`, `_agents`, `_agent`) too, whose hooks and MCP servers it runs outside any sandbox; Copilot's in `.github` (`.github/hooks`, which it runs without asking, `.github/copilot`, `.github/mcp.json`, `.github/lsp.json`, `.github/extensions`, `.github/agents`), only where a `.github` folder exists, so that the folder can still be made; and Muse Code's `.muse`, whose hooks it runs outside its sandbox in a workspace it trusts. Also the conversation's other working directories (`extraCwds`): written as the first in edit mode only, each one's project agent config read-only, and each refused in edit mode where the first would be. A folder the run may write that overlaps voice input's folder is refused naming that folder. And the shell runs and the probe give sandbox-runtime (`sandboxShell()`, `probeHostConfig()`): `sh`, which, running the sandbox's command as the host is told to with a pipe of Node's as stdin and no `SHLVL`, reads no `.bashrc` of the agent's home, so the agent keeps its own `HOME` (with bash it would not on Linux, #117); the team's protected paths kept from being written: what is there by name on both systems, globs on macOS only.
- `test/sandboxProcs.test.ts` — finding a relay-sandbox run's processes on macOS by their sandbox: this user's processes from `ps`, the kernel asked through the system Python about the run's own folder against the folder around it, nothing told off macOS or without the command line tools (the marker sweep is then all there is at a run's teardown; the server's start keeps the folders killed runs left), a folder that is gone, kills in rounds until none is left (a process that forks while it is killed), and the Apple-silicon calling convention for the variadic `sandbox_check`.
- `test/relaySandbox-queued-ask.test.ts` — a network ask the relay sandbox queued behind another is refused, never put to the user, once the turn has ended (with the real orchestrator: no card left pending, no directory lock left taken).
- `test/relaySandbox-leftovers.test.ts` — on macOS, disposing of a run sweeps what still holds its marker before removing it (the host may have been killed before its own sweep), and the startup sweep finds holders by the marker; a run that fails in `sandboxCommand` leaves no marker behind.
- `test/opencode.test.ts` — the OpenCode adapter: turns replayed from messages recorded with the real CLI (`test/fixtures/opencode/`, by a fake `opencode` in `test/fake-opencode.ts`: text, thinking, tools and their rows, the to-do list as the plan, approvals per permission, `fs/write_text_file` answered and never done, cancel, resume and a failed resume, a bad key and bad models, MCP servers); its home (variables, the read-only config folder kept as written, console accounts, sessions' permission rules and saved permissions removed from its database), the run's config per backend and permission (escaped `{env:}` / `{file:}`), the project's instruction files, the order of mode, model and effort, refusal reasons handed over through its server, the turn's tokens and cost from its database (the `step-finish` parts of the turn's model calls, a subagent's sessions' too, those from before the turn or a stopped one left out, the cache and reasoning added in; the parts there before a turn read without their data, as one JSON array of ids (text ids only; a session with more than 200,000 given up on), the turn's added up in SQL (numbers past 2^63, 1e400, text and negative values); a view, a link or a named pipe in place of the database or its log refused, by the scrub too; built with `test/opencode-db.ts`), its errors, its catalog and what it loads by itself. Also the session permission rules and saved permissions taken out of its database before each run, with the triggers and views planted to put them back dropped (a generated column that keeps them throws), and the member's state started over, empty, when they cannot be taken out; a line continuation and a `function NAME` header read as OpenCode reads them; and `relay-shell` looking for the evaluating forms outside single quotes only where they can be told apart (`awk -F= '/^[A-Z_]+=/ {print $2}'` runs; a declaration's or `let`'s quoted argument, a quote in double quotes or a comment, and one left open are still screened; `let`, and `$(( … ))` beside a bracketed `$`, are evaluating forms, while `$(( … ))` beside a quoted `$` alone runs). Also refused below full access on Linux before anything starts (its server could not be watched there). On Windows it keeps its own shell (no `relay-shell` in its config). Also the sandbox's connect-only ports: those of the HTTP MCP servers on this computer it is given (Harness Relay's gateway among them). Also the member's limit as its `build` agent's `steps`. Also an endpoint model's context window and output limit as its `limit` (a quarter of the window for an answer when no output limit is set; context 0 with an output limit alone), and for the run's model `compaction.prune` and tool output limits at an eighth of the window, left out from 136,536 tokens and without a window.
- `test/orchestrator-workspace.test.ts` — the working directory with edit permission: an edit-mode turn refused in Harness Relay's own folder (also for a conversation saved before the check), parallel worktree isolation and apply, change tracking in roundtable mode (also of a turn whose agent committed its work itself), and two conversations writing in the same folder at once: the second one's team in a worktree of its own (named as the folder is, on the team's branch, every member in it, told so in its briefing and a note after the user's message, browsed as the team's), its changes there and the first one's only its own, Apply refused while the first one writes and applied once it is done, the next run in the folder again with the worktree, its folder and branch removed; its next runs kept there while what it did is not applied, also after a restart, until a resend past it drops it; a retry of a later reply keeping it as it is (the first reply's change applied with it); a team that changed nothing there back in the folder, or, while the folder is still taken, given a copy made again from the folder as it is now; also for a repository and one nested inside it; read-only conversations reading meanwhile; and kept snapshot refs dropped with the messages that linked to them and with the conversation.
- `test/orchestrator-folders.test.ts` — several working directories: every agent gets them all (`addDirs`), each becomes a repository and a turn records its changes in each, with one diff naming each folder; another conversation writing in one of them meanwhile works in worktrees of its own of all of them (the first as `cwd`, the others as `addDirs`, all of its result applied or none); in parallel mode each agent gets a worktree of each, an apply applies all of them or, with a conflict in one, none; and the worktrees of a folder taken out go at the next parallel run. One folder inside another becomes one repository whichever comes first; a worktree of a folder taken off the list is neither applied nor diffed, also once that folder is gone; and the second repository's kept snapshots go with the conversation. It takes the lock on all of them for an action allowed in a read-only run, sends a schedule's message while another conversation writes in one (its team in worktrees of its own), records the others' changes when one cannot be snapshotted, and marks the worktrees for a reset when a patch fails after another was applied.
- `test/orchestrator-team.test.ts` — the lead agent: auto team with a fake lead agent (a custom design that waits for confirmation, then its team answers the question with what was written meanwhile; a team set by hand as confirmation; saving it as a template; using a template; the fallback, keeping the current team when there is one; a design for each new question with the conversation so far and the current team, members who stay on keeping their sessions; the current team taking an answer, a message that @mentions a member, Continue and a retry; a team used at once without confirmation, and one that cannot start (a note, then Continue answers without a new design); the current team kept while a design waits (a retry of an earlier reply, declining the design, none to keep for a first question); confirming refused while the conversation is held; an interjection no turn took and an edit that @mentions a member going to the current team, while "stop and ask this instead" designs; Continue designing again after a stopped design; designing again after a question is edited, with what came before it as history; and planning runs that lock nothing), and the directory lock once its team is confirmed, by that team's mode (another conversation then working in its own worktree). Also a design keeps the tools and account the user set for a member who stays on, and gives new members their CLI's defaults whatever tools the lead names; the lead agent's context gathered for the conversation's folder when it designs, and for the drawer's folder when it changes a team, with each CLI's tools in its prompt. Also the lead agent changing a team at the user's request (`reviseTeam`: the whole team returned, with the request and the conversation in its prompt, members who stay keeping their ids, nothing changed in the conversation, a waiting design left waiting; the drawer's permission and folder read while the saved folder is used (the same folder spelled another way is not another one); the schedule's message only while the schedule is on; a waiting design for the schedule, its message shown although the schedule is off; a member renamed with `was` keeping its id; a declined change keeping the team; empty or unusable requests and replies, and a bad name in the drawer's team, refused; the lead's CLI failing; its session linked in AgentsView; stopped by a closed request, a newer one, or deleting the conversation; a replaced request waited for, on delete too, until its lead process has ended; stopped on shutdown and waited for until its lead process has ended, with new requests refused then). Also a team saved before the team limit was lowered handed to the lead agent to change (the answer cut to the limit; a draft larger than both refused). Also sorting a new conversation into a group with a fake lead agent (by its first message only, next to the team, not without groups, never over the user's move, stopped when the conversation is deleted). Auto team also covers an exhausted lead account, reassignment to a different account at execution, no eligible models with queued messages, recovery on Continue, and a deleted private endpoint. It also verifies isolated model replies, deduplication, replacement, full-access confirmation, endpoint boundaries and streamed lead activity.
- `test/orchestrator-branch.test.ts` — branching a conversation (the copied messages with new ids, the team, settings and group, fresh sessions given the copied history; while the team replies but not past a reply still being written; copied attachments and kept diffs that outlive the original; the branch point moved back by a retry and dropped by an edit; a question the reply asked kept open, also once answered there, in each mode (in relay, with the teammates the reply handed to after it); not while a parallel peer's turn still records its changes; deleting the original and shutdown waiting for the files being copied, a missing one skipped, the copies readable by the owner only; the browser's screenshots a copied reply shows copied with it, its text pointed at the copies, which show also once the original is gone; a parallel reply's images from the worktree it was made in; a branch named after the first message written there, also one that waited in its queue, only while it has the name it was made with, in either language; renamed after an edit that leaves nothing copied, unless the user named it; a branch made while its original was being sorted taken along into its group; a branch from a reply a branch had copied, the branch point and the end of an earlier part included, made a branch of the conversation that reply was written in, two levels up too, named after it by its title now and listed under it, with the parts before it kept (`earlier`); once that conversation is deleted, by the title the sidebar shows for it, with its `via`, also when it is deleted while the branch's files are copied, so the family stays together; a branch left with nothing of the conversation it was branched from by a retry or an edit made one of the conversation further up, renamed after its title now unless the user named it, and no longer a branch once nothing copied is left; `branchOrigin()` and `branchAfterCut()` on their own, a part that cannot be placed left out).
- `test/orchestrator-checks.test.ts` — team checks with the fake CLI: the card after the replies with who changed what and its baseline kept, the rules in the briefing and what failed in the next turn (the latest result only, once), none when the run cannot write or the list is empty, a pass with nothing to check where no commit was made, the queue held while it fails (Skip lets it go, and it is not the team's to fix any more), Check again, Ask a member to fix it, a result gone stale, the lead review reading it, parallel mode, a team in the conversation's own worktree (checked there, after each turn, not what another conversation changes in the folder meanwhile; no commit format, said so in the briefing; its commands at the apply; Check again likewise; a result that did not pass in the folder checked in the copy from where its run began, an ignored protected file not taken for removed; the team's result held before it is applied, naming no member, and applied anyway), a run that stopped to ask, a schedule's run nobody watched failing its time, and a template's list copied only into a conversation without one; and commands: run once the team has finished with the end of a failing one's output in the next turn, what one changed itself, below full access through the sandbox, held back when the run changed what they read (until confirmed; `package.json` only for its scripts), none when the team stopped to ask or in parallel mode, Check again running them in the background (busy, a message waits, Stop), and Try it; and what the independent review found: Stop while a command runs (could not run, nothing left running), a message sent during Try it going once it is done, Try it refused with read-only permission, deleting the chat stopping a command Check again runs, Check again running none in parallel mode or read-only, commands held back in later runs until confirmed and nothing for agents to fix meanwhile, a changed script found from the folder its command runs in, and each command listed once when the paths could not be checked; and from the second review: a stopped command holding nothing nor reaching the next turn, the gate carried across a run that stopped to ask, a script named by a full path, the commands reported when the checks could not start, shutdown waiting for Check again's command, and Try it with full access as the drawer shows it; and applying a parallel result: a protected file holding it back and Apply anyway, the commands run in a check tree with the result and never in the working directory, the installed dependencies linked in with their caches kept apart, a failing command holding one result while another is applied, Stop applying nothing, and native Windows applying it without running commands; and from the review of applying: a result that changes what the commands read held back until confirmed, the sandbox with full access when it starts, Apply anyway only for the result its card checked, and no check tree on native Windows; a template's rules added to Settings' default copied into a chat, but not to a list changed since, and a template without rules leaving it. Also the team's hooks: given to a member on a CLI that runs them, in its own folder, a refusal going to the agent and into the reply, and the briefing telling them; none in read-only, and none for a member on another CLI, whose reply says so. Also the app preview rule (an app a turn made that cannot run, why, in the next turn's prompt, a pass once it runs, no card when it is the only rule and no turn made an app) and fixing by itself (the member the finding names, the message's mark and text, `via="team-checks"`, kept in the database, at most three in a row and the user's own Fix starting the count again and marked as theirs, nothing after Stop, while the team waits for the user's answer or one of their messages waits, or for a command that could not run, and a fix that cannot start ending the run's status); an app that fails staying failing after a turn that touched none of its files (Check again too), a library's `package.json` and a dependency folder counting for nothing, and the apply card checking the applied member's apps again.
- `test/orchestrator-steer.test.ts` — steering with the fake runner (`fake.steer`, `fake.refuseSteer`): off by default (the message waits in the queue); in roundtable mode into the speaking member's turn, posted at once after its reply, marked `queued` and `steered`, seen by that member (not in its next prompt) and by the others as any message, kept in the database; several sent at once go in in order; in relay mode into the member holding the relay, while one for another member waits for them; a refusal falls back to the queue; a message with files, or one sent while another waits, waits too; a failed turn offers it again; in parallel mode into every turn of the round, and not when one of the round cannot take it or has finished, nor when it names members of the round; stopped while it is being sent into a turn, it waits in the queue and starts no run; switched on and off while the team replies (`setSteer`).
- `test/steer.test.ts` — steering CLI by CLI (`STEER_PROVIDERS`): the steer prompt; Claude Code's user message (`priority: "next"`, a uuid), taken on its `command_lifecycle`, refused, withdrawn (`cancel_async_message`) when it says nothing, and the input kept open while one waits or more turns follow (`ClaudeSteering`), and with the stand-in claude (`FAKE_CLAUDE_STEER`) folded into the turn, run after a result as a turn of its own, or refused; Codex's `turn/steer`; Grok's `_x.ai/interject` and its answers; Antigravity's further `session/prompt` (taken once an earlier prompt answers or the grace time passes, the last result and all the tokens, a failed prompt failing the turn); Goose's `_goose/unstable/session/steer`; Pi's `steer` command.
- `test/orchestrator-approve.test.ts` — "Approve for me" with a fake lead agent: what it approves allowed without asking, the card saying so with its reason, saved; the review read-only, never asking, without a state key, reading the user's words and the request; what it flags waiting for the user with its reason, and allowed once only when the answer says always (no rule); no waiting (nor alerts) while it reviews, and the user answering first stopping it; a review that fails or gives no decision asking the user; pushing never reviewed; the same request again in a run not reviewed again, a new run reviewing it; "always allow" answering first; no review when the conversation asks or refuses; the agents' briefing saying it. (A schedule's run, in `orchestrator-schedule.test.ts`: what it approves allowed, what it flags or never reviews refused, the card saying why; a schedule that asks waiting for the user.)
- `test/orchestrator-memory.test.ts` — long-term memory in team runs with the fake CLI: a reply's blocks taken out of its text into a `memory` part and recorded (where it holds, how it is known, who wrote it and where), the next teammate reading what it saved with its id, every member's briefing saying what memory is for, a reply that only saved a note not a pass, and one that passed keeping its card and telling the next teammate; a failed reply keeping the notes it finished writing; a later conversation given the pinned notes in its briefing and a matching note in its turn prompt once per session (not one of its own, nor one of another folder; its id in `seen`, when it was given recorded), and again after a turn that failed; a note's card following the user's changes; Retry setting aside the notes of the reply it takes away; memory switched off (the blocks stay, nothing in the briefing); the lead agent tidying once 5 notes changed (read-only, a state of its own emptied, the notes and the record they came from), its merge, removal and pin applied and the cards updated, and not again so soon; which switches stop a tidying (memory off; not review, nor automatic tidying for the user's own); a failed tidying recorded, and Tidy up now refused while it is off, tidying or has nothing to tidy.
- `test/orchestrator-review.test.ts` — lead review with a fake lead agent: the review once the team has answered (read-only, in a state of its own, after every reply), what its prompt holds (the user's message, the numbered replies, the files a turn changed), its card (summary, highlights about the replies by id, a quote the reply does not have dropped, unknown kinds as notes, next steps, the lead's cost) and the setting saved; the lead never given a way to ask for an approval, so it can take no directory lock while it reviews; a review under way stopped when it is switched off (its card aborted, no "Stopped" note, the run ending as usual and the schedule's message that waited answered; switched on again, nothing more to review); the switch refused for a conversation being deleted; none when the conversation does not ask for it, nor in a run where nobody answered (every member passed or failed); none while the team waits for the user's answer, then one that reads the question and the answer; each review reading only what came after the last one, with the conversation before as context; a failure (the CLI's, or an answer that is no review) said on the card, and those replies read again next time; Stop stopping it (the card aborted, no "Stopped" note, the replies left to the next review); a message sent while it reviews stopping it and going at once (not queued), the team answering it and the next review reading both, also a message of files alone, but not Continue, which is refused (409) while the review goes on; a branch's copied review pointing at the copied replies, and a branch starting with the setting its conversation has when it is branched, not the one it had then. Also a first review turned on mid-chat reading only the latest question; what the last review said given to the lead; the members' worktrees (parallel mode) and the other working directories given as its other folders; the working directory free for another conversation while it reviews; counted by `busy()`, its session linked in AgentsView, stopped by deleting the conversation; one review after a message that waited for a run of its own, reading both runs; reading again from the earlier boundary after a retry dropped a review; turned on and off at any time, also while the team replies, decided once the team has answered; the chat a schedule opens each time copying the setting, as it copies the other settings (on, that chat is reviewed; switched off in the schedule's conversation, the next one has it off). Also "Skip the review and send now" stopping the review and sending what waits (a schedule's message) to the team, the next review reading from where the stopped one started; and a branch keeping where a failed review started. Also, with auto team, a team designed for a message sent while it reviews (with Enter or Cmd+Enter), which stops the review first, as for any new question; the members a message sent then @mentions answering it at once, with no new design; a message sent while it reviews when another conversation took the folder meanwhile going all the same, the review stopped and the team in its own worktree, which the next review is told of; "Skip the review and send now" the same way; the folder kept while the review is stopped for a message, so another conversation that starts meanwhile works in its own worktree; a review stopped before it started read from after a restart (its card back with where it started reading); the latest question read when it is turned on again after an old review; a relay reviewed after its hand-offs. Also schedules during a review, and Retry and Edit after a stopped one: a schedule's message that comes due during a review queued unmarked and left to the current team, answered after the review with no design and reviewed; a schedule's message that waits for a review sent with the message the user sends meanwhile, which stops the review (with auto team, the design's question holds both); a schedule's message tried again later (`busy`), rather than queued into a design that will wait for confirmation; a Retry and an Edit after a stopped review reviewed, reading their replies and those the stopped reviews left; Retry, Edit, Apply and a settings change refused (409) while the lead agent reviews; a review stopped before other questions not read back to; shutting down stopping a review, and none starting once the server is stopping. And lead review the user's alone: a design never turns it on (the lead agent's prompt says nothing of it, whatever its reply says), and the user's switch holds for the question being answered and every later one, designs leaving it as it is. And notifications while the lead reviews (the server's watcher, `AlertWatcher`, over the conversations' list): "finished" only once the review has ended, about the team's last reply and not the review, also after a review that failed (done, not an error), was switched off or was stopped; an error (the team's last reply failed) after the review; once, after the next review, when a message sent during a review stops it, when "Skip the review and send now" sends what waited, and when a message sent during the team's turn waits for a run of its own (the run before it not reviewed), with a check taken while the next run is set up (`prepareWorkspace()`, where one run hands over to the next, the conversation running there, in the list too) telling of nothing; and an approval, a question and a team to confirm told at once, with "finished" after the review that follows; a question told once, also when the run that takes the answer was at work at a check and then could not be set up (the page's rule, `AlertTracker`, too).
- `test/orchestrator-budgets.test.ts` — the lead agent's answers in the shape its CLI is asked for (the design, the review, a change on request and the sidebar group each from its structured answer, with the schema it was asked for, the text read without one), a member's own limit reaching its run (only its CLI's), and what a failed or stopped turn spent kept with its reply.
- `test/orchestrator-schedule.test.ts` — schedules (`Scheduler` with a fake clock): the message sent when it comes due, marked as scheduled and shown to the agents as such, once only, and the next time; approvals refused in the runs it starts (shown as declined cards) except what the chat always allows, unless the schedule asks; asked again once the user writes into a scheduled run, or when a scheduled send also delivers the user's own waiting message; waiting in the queue while the team replies and holding a second one back, or a full queue; an earlier one left waiting after Stop sent at the next time, once; a run of its own for a message no turn took, still refusing approvals; held back while the team waits for an answer (also with an earlier one waiting) or has no confirmed team, times held back one after another for the same reason sharing one note that counts them (saved, and the chat told of it each time as the same message; while the team waits for an answer and while a run goes on), and a note of its own after something else is written, a time sent between (a new chat each time, by the scheduler, Run now or the webhook; any time not skipped, sent, queued or failed, while one that is busy or skipped does not), a time dropped between (as missed, while the computer slept, say), the schedule saved again (also through the HTTP API with its times kept, and switched off and on), another reason, or a restart (a new orchestrator), and the note forgotten with its conversation, taken by the current team of an auto-team conversation without a design, and never taken for the answer (a reply, Continue, a scheduled @mention), staying queued after an answer until the agent it is for speaks (roundtable and relay), while the user's own message waiting behind it goes with the answer (a typed answer, Continue, a scheduled @mention for another agent); a missed time skipped (with a note) or sent once late, also for a repeating schedule after several misses, and a one-time schedule switched off; retried while a change being made holds the conversation (also when already late), then given up after the retry window; sent while another conversation writes in the same folder, its team in a worktree of its own; refused without cards in a conversation that refuses automatically; asked again in parallel mode once the user writes; nothing recorded when the server stops mid-send; a schedule replaced mid-send left as it is; an unreadable stored schedule dropped; a run that cannot start; and nothing for a deleted conversation. A schedule that opens a new chat each time: a new conversation with the same team, settings, "always allow" answers and group (not sorted by the lead agent), named after the time, with a note and the message, and new agent sessions each time; approvals refused there unless the schedule asks, and asked once the user writes; sent whatever the schedule's own conversation is doing (waiting for an answer, replying); sent while that or another conversation writes in the same folder, the new chat's team in a worktree of its own; no chat without a confirmed team, none left behind when its run cannot start; "Run now" saying which chat it opened; the chats named after the message while the schedule's conversation has no name; the link forgotten when that chat is deleted; a chat removed when the server begins to stop while it is set up; a chat from a conversation in Other left unsorted; parallel worktrees of its own. A team the lead agent designs for a schedule (`designForSchedule`): for its message, which nobody posts, told it is a schedule (here or in a new chat each time); nothing runs once it is confirmed, used at once (`autoConfirm`) or set by hand, but what the user wrote while it waited is answered; the schedule's message then goes to that team; a time due while the team is designed or waits tried again (`busy`), then sent once it is confirmed; a later question in that chat designed for as usual; no design without a schedule or auto team, while one waits, or over a team.
- `test/orchestrator-gateway.test.ts` — Harness Relay's own tools in a turn (also what RLM's sub-model spent through a CLI added to the reply's tokens and cost, only where the member's CLI reports a cost of its own) (a stand-in gateway): its MCP server given for the groups the member's CLI has (none without, none when the member turns them off), the grant for that turn alone, its calls' signal ended with the turn however it ends (also a CLI that timed out, while the run goes on with the next member); the CLI's own question about one of its tools (Claude Code's name, Codex's `relay:<tool>`) answered yes without a card, also in a conversation that refuses every question, where any other question is refused as before; Grok, Muse Code and the open agents given them in full access only; a send call's card in the reply, an equal call adding none, the chat waiting for the user, and their Send sending it once, with a note; a reply taken back (Retry) discarding what it had waiting, the reply that comes again asking anew; a branch copying no waiting card; the card of an item a stop of the server cut off while sending brought up to date at the next start. Also how each CLI is given it: over HTTP for OpenCode, Codex and Copilot (with the stdio stand-in only where it could run), none below full access for Grok, Muse Code, Goose, Cline, OpenHands and Pi, and the stdio program for the rest. Also OpenCode's question about a gateway tool (`relay_<tool>`) answered in a conversation that refuses every question, and a library server named `relay_…` left out of a run that has the gateway. And a hand-over's card in the reply (the chat waiting while it is pending or in the user's hands, expired with the turn), none in a run a schedule started.
- `test/orchestrator-watch.test.ts` — automations that start when something happens (`Scheduler` with a fake clock and a stand-in for the source): the first check records and sends nothing; something new sends the message once, its `<event source="mail">` block as data in the agents' prompt and its source kept with the message; a restart (a new store and scheduler) does not send it again; a failed check kept, the next ones slower, cleared once one works, the sidebar told; new mail the team cannot take now left for a later check, which sends it with what came meanwhile; an automation that is off checked only on Check now, one check at a time, and then only recording; one changed while it was checked left to its new settings. The scheduler's own look checks it (no test call); new mail while the team replies queued with its source; a new chat each time; a message that could not be sent noted in the chat and counted as seen; mail held for a team that still cannot take it not asked for again; mail counted as seen when the automation was saved while its message went; Check now asking the server while mail is held, the held reason in the automation's language, and when mail was last seen (`seenAt`) kept by held and failing checks; a source checked with the conversation's working directory and the check's time, and only what each source keeps kept, the events and files it saw left out of what browsers get (`forClient()`). Files in the working directory with the real file source and the fake CLI writing in it: a report from outside starts a run; what the run writes, at a check while it writes and at the first check after it, is only recorded (`teamWrote`), so it never starts another; a file from outside then starts one again; a file held for the team still sent after a run of its starts and ends; what is written while a check is held or fails taken as the team's, and a read-only run as no writer; a run that began and ended between two checks counted by the folder's write count, and a read-only run's allowed action as writing; held files never reaching browsers; a file that arrives while another waits for the team held with it; writes kept by another start of the server, and only the watched folder's, counted; what a run that begins during a check writes, still settling, taken for the team's while what already waited stays kept; why held files wait still said through a check that only records. While the mail server says when mail arrives (IMAP IDLE): checks every 15 minutes, held mail still after 2, `listening` only while it listens, one more check after the one under way when IDLE asks, and the automations IDLE listens for (on, waiting for new mail); one more check after the one under way even when that one failed (a deleted conversation's 404). Several things to wait for: each checked on its own, its own state, next check and slower checks after its errors, the others' going on; a message for each that finds something (two in one check, the second queued), each with its own block; only the triggers whose next check has come checked at the scheduler's look; a restart reading each back; IMAP IDLE's ask (`Scheduler.mailArrived()`, as the server makes it) checking only its folder's trigger (also as one more check after one under way, the asks put together), each trigger listening on its own, and Check now after such a check checking every one; a trigger removed while it was checked never written back nor taken for an error, one added checked at once; a second file trigger's file still sent in the same check after the first one's message started a run in the folder, while what still settles then is not kept for later; the folder taken as it was just before the message that started a run went, so what a run the user started during a slow mail check wrote is the team's, only recorded; a conversation deleted during a check having its other triggers left unchecked; what browsers get of each trigger's checks, without what they saw (`scheduleForClient()`); a check looking at its file triggers first, so a file a run another trigger's message started moves in with its old time during a slower check is never sent.
- `test/orchestrator-tracking.test.ts` — a schedule that tracks a goal (`Scheduler` and the orchestrator on a fake clock): the goal and the latest notes in each run's briefing, a reply's `<progress>` block kept as a `progress` part and a note (before or after an ask), `done` switching the schedule off with a note, `blocked` keeping it on, the notes carried into the chat a schedule opens each time; a time whose replies all failed tried again after 5, 30 and 120 minutes, then left to the next time, a retry left to the next time when that comes first, none after Stop; a run a restart cut off tried again at startup, also one the server stopped while shutting down; none for a run the schedule no longer has in flight (switched off meanwhile) nor for Run now; a reply that only reports on the goal counted as said, not a pass; a run that could not start tried again; a webhook's body given to the agents as an `<event>` block and never retried; nothing of a goal without one; a reply to the user's own message in a tracking chat not told the goal, and reporting nothing on it; a scheduled run that waited in the queue, or for the user's answer, still tracked (its reply reports, and a failed one is tried again); Retry and Continue of a tracked run tracked again, without a retry of their own; a retry left alone once a note was recorded after it came due.

Also the tool library's MCP servers in ACP's form (the helpers in `acp.ts`, which Cursor shares). HTTP MCP servers only for a Grok that advertises them. Also the variables that leave out what Grok loads from Claude Code and Cursor (skills, MCP servers, hooks), and its memory, next to the user's variables and the sandbox (`harness-relay` in read-only and edit mode, `workspace` on Windows, none set with full access). On the user's own endpoint: the models' block of `config.toml` (`grokModelsBlock()`: hidden, the key from `HARNESS_RELAY_ENDPOINT_API_KEY`; placed after the user's settings, replaced in place, taken out with the last endpoint), written only once a turn uses it, with the file's mode and through a link, taken as it is by the turns running when it changes, and a run with a stand-in `grok agent stdio`: the model by Grok's id for it, no effort, the endpoint's key. Also a model typed for an endpoint that its list leaves out getting a table of its own for its run, and a model of a deleted endpoint failing before Grok starts. Also the table of a typed model kept by every write of the block in that home while a run using it is under way, until the last such run ends, and only in that home. Only for an endpoint still set up; and in a whole run at full access the table stays while the turn streams, released only once it is over. A run with other working directories gets a profile of its own named after them (`read_write`), beside the main one in `sandbox.toml`, only the latest 32 kept; another conversation's profile written during a turn is not taken for a command's change, while a command's write in any block still is. A profile a running turn uses is never dropped; `grokRunner.run` writes the run's own profile into Grok's home and starts Grok in it (a stand-in `grok`), then lets it go. A later turn's own folder profile, written after putting back what an earlier turn's command changed in `sandbox.toml`, or by a write that failed part way, is Harness Relay's: neither put back nor named as a change, nor is a `config.toml` link leading nowhere that its write replaced with a file; a command's change made while it writes still is. Tables of its models that Grok kept outside the block when it wrote its config again (its markers dropped) are taken out, so none is defined twice, also when no turn adds the block (which then comes back up to date). Also Harness Relay's gateway over HTTP: its stdio stand-in where it has one, else left out, when Grok takes no HTTP server. Also its subagents and background work, in the kinds Grok 1.0.92's binary writes: a background `spawn_subagent` call's subagent's calls (on its own session) under the call, which ends with `subagent_finished`'s output or error, a foreground one with its own result; its notes (`auto_compact_*`, `auto_recovery_*`, `model_auto_switched`); the wait after the prompt answers (`idle()`), until its background subagents and tasks are done and the turn it wakes Grok for has ended, or Grok has been quiet, what is still at work then cut off; a run with a stand-in that streams the woken turn into the same reply; and the caps on its subagents (`GROK_SUBAGENT_CAPS`), only where they are on.

What it refuses (no turn, code missing or too long, a `timeout_sec` out of range), native Windows, no Node.js, a sandbox that does not start, and as many runs at once as it may. Its sandbox: read-only, no host allowed and nobody asked, the protected paths and other runs' folders unreadable, the run's folders removed when it cannot start. Through a real gateway's loopback HTTP endpoint: offered with the turn's tools, its code reaching only those and never `run_code` itself, an act asked about with a card saying code asked (refused when nobody can be asked), a send put in the outbox, nothing called once the turn is over. What its code cannot do: end the result's block with a call's name, a note or a key of `state` (each cut short), make the result grow past its limits in a loop, pass a call whose arguments are too long, have a line that is not the protocol's taken as one (a forged end is the code's end), harm the server by writing without end while ignoring SIGTERM; a run stopped when its caller stops waiting, or once it has waited its limit for the user's answers while its code keeps running; the questions still being answered when its process ends without a result, aborted; the calls the code did not wait for made and listed once it returned, and none of a run that did not finish. Offered only while Settings → Labs has a mode on (not listed, nor callable, when off), never annotated read-only, and said to be unavailable when the sandbox's last check failed. A question a tool asks by itself (the browser about a new host) counted with the others, its clock stopped meanwhile, and one past the limit answered no without asking, the code told why (also through a real gateway, its card saying code asked); a call the code left behind no longer waited for once the wait's limit is up, listed as not done with the ones after it. The sandbox checked only once the tool is offered, never when it is made (a check as the server starts would race its sweep of the sandbox's folders, and its failure, kept a while, would refuse the open agents' runs). Also the sub-model's tokens counted into the turn (`GatewayContext.usage`) once each question is answered, and added to the reply's only where its CLI reports its own (`withSubModelTokens()`). Through a CLI: the description says each question starts that CLI, 4 are answered at once through one CLI over every run (another CLI not held up), and a CLI's cost is added to the reply's only where the member's CLI reports one.

Also that no bare address in the zh-TW docs runs straight into the Chinese text after it, which GitHub would link along with it (write `<http://…>`).
- `test/devcontainer.test.ts` — the dev container for GitHub Codespaces (`.devcontainer/`): it forwards the port `npm start` listens on and runs its own scripts; its container options are the ones bubblewrap needs and no more (no `privileged`, capabilities, mounts or `init`; only the sshd and GitHub CLI features); `setup.sh` runs in `updateContentCommand` (so also at each update of a prebuild) and installs the seven CLIs that are not open source (npm's, and the makers' own installers fetched whole, again after a failure, given no input, and linked into `/usr/local/bin`) and no open-source agent; run with stand-ins for `sudo`, `curl`, `npm` and `mktemp`, it starts npm's install before the first installer and lets it run while the makers' installers run one after another, shows npm's output after theirs, fails when npm's install fails (once the rest is installed), fails when an installer cannot be fetched or fails and stops npm's install then (with the installer's status, also when npm is done already), and leaves nothing in `TMPDIR`; `start.sh` run with stand-ins for `npm` and `curl` asks the server's default address (`DEFAULT_HOST`) whether one runs, starts it with the codespace's address in `RELAY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` and its sign-in link (and the server's stderr) on that address with the code beside it, or prints `npm run login`'s link there without Vite's; it keeps the hosts already allowed, reads only the names it asks for from Codespaces' file in a shell without its profile, and changes nothing outside a codespace. The create page recommends no secrets. `count.sh` (its `postCreateCommand`, never in a prebuild), run in a checkout of its own with a stand-in `curl`: one empty `POST` to the website's mirror's `/codespaces` (connecting 3 s at most, 5 s in all), and a mark in `data/` (git-ignored) so that a rebuild does not count again; only for this repository (`GITHUB_REPOSITORY`) on `latest-release` (the Release workflow's `CODESPACES_BRANCH`), never a fork's or another branch's, and nothing with `MIRROR` empty; tried again when the mirror could not count it, and never a failure; nothing with `DO_NOT_TRACK` set (but to `0`), which it says, and nothing outside a codespace (with `bash`; skipped on Windows).

Good next candidates:

- **Adapter parsers** — record real CLI output into fixture files and assert the emitted `AgentEvent`s. To capture output:

  ```bash
  echo "hello" | claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages > claude.jsonl
  # Codex, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code speak JSON-RPC; log what the adapter sees with a console.log in
  # JsonRpcConnection.handleLine, or copy Grok's per-session updates from
  # ~/.grok/sessions/<url-encoded cwd>/<session-id>/updates.jsonl
  ```

- **Prompt helpers** — `buildTurnPrompt`.
- **More orchestrator scheduling** — relay hand-offs and `[PASS]` early exit, with the same fake runner as the orchestrator's tests (`test/orchestrator-harness.ts`).

Running real agents in tests costs money and needs logins, so keep automated tests offline. Tests never touch the real credential store either: `SettingsStore` takes it as a constructor argument, and only `src/server/index.ts` passes `openSystemKeyring()`.

## End-to-end tests

```bash
npm run test:e2e:install                  # once: the Chromium Playwright drives (Linux: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium)
npm run test:e2e                          # every spec in e2e/specs/, in Chromium
npx playwright test e2e/specs/chat.spec.ts
npx playwright test -g approvals          # by title
npx playwright test --ui                  # pick and watch tests in Playwright's window
npx playwright show-report                # the last run's report, with the traces of failed tests
E2E_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit npm run test:e2e   # after npx playwright install firefox webkit
```

They drive the UI in a real browser ([Playwright](https://playwright.dev), [`playwright.config.ts`](../playwright.config.ts)) against the real server, `src/server/index.ts` with the built UI, so the routes, sign-in, SSE, the orchestrator, the adapters and the workspace's git are tried together, as a person uses them. The agents are fake CLIs, so the tests stay offline and cost nothing, and they run the same on macOS, Linux and Windows. [`e2e/README.md`](../e2e/README.md) lists every scenario they try, area by area, with what each one checks, which CLIs it runs with and what they do not cover yet; change it (in both languages) with every end-to-end test you add, change or remove.
