- **Each test has a server of its own** (`e2e/support/relay.ts`, the `relay` fixture in [`e2e/fixtures.ts`](../e2e/fixtures.ts)): on a free port (`PORT=0`), with a temporary folder for its data (`RELAY_DATA_DIR`), a home (`HOME`, `USERPROFILE`, and the XDG folders in it, so Cursor's home is its `.config/cursor`), the CLIs' homes (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `GROK_HOME`, `COPILOT_HOME` (its `.copilot`), and Antigravity's `.gemini` and Muse Code's XDG folders in that home; the open agents' are in the data folder, `agent-homes/<cli>`) and the default working directory (a small project, `seedProject()`), and `RELAY_KEYRING=off`: nothing of your own Harness Relay, CLIs or keychain is read or changed. On macOS and Linux that folder is in `/tmp` (`E2E_TMP_DIR` names another), as Harness Relay's own sandbox needs a short data folder for its sockets and macOS's temporary folder is too deep; a test server refuses to start where its data folder would be too long. Your credentials are left out of its environment too (`ANTHROPIC_*`, `OPENAI_*`, `XAI_*`, `GROK_*`, `CODEX_*`, `GH_*`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and any `*_API_KEY` or `*_TOKEN`), and so are Cursor's, Antigravity's and Copilot's own (`CURSOR_API_KEY`, `CURSOR_AUTH_TOKEN`, `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`, `CURSOR_INVOKED_AS`, `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE`, `NO_OPEN_BROWSER`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `AGY_ADC_AUTH`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, `ANTIGRAVITY_*`), Copilot's (`COPILOT_*`: its home, model, BYOK endpoint, allow-all), your own XDG folders, and the runner's and your Harness Relay's variables (`PLAYWRIGHT_*`, `E2E_*`, `RELAY_*`, Claude Code's nesting ones), so a real CLI or `gh` found by mistake has nothing to reach a service with. Git looks for a repository no further up than the test's folder (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`), so one around it, such as a home kept in git, is never taken for the working directory's. Through the API, before the page opens, the CLIs a test does not need (`missingClis`: by default all but Claude Code, Codex and Grok, `DEFAULT_CLIS` in `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts`, as the server asks every installed CLI for its version, sign-in, models and what it loads by itself whenever a page opens, some six starts each, so that ten CLIs made a test a second slower on CI, three seconds on Windows) are given an executable that does not exist, so they are not installed there (`eachCli()`, `eachHalf()` and `withClis()` install the CLIs a test is about, `withEveryCli()` all of them); then the test's fake model endpoint (below) becomes a model backend and its first model the open agents' default, and OpenHands' terminal switch is turned on. When the test ends the server is stopped and its folder removed. When that fails (Harness Relay makes some folders in it read-only, such as OpenCode's config in its home), the owner's write permission is given back on every folder under it, without following a link, and the removal is tried again; a folder that still cannot be removed is only reported (`[e2e] could not remove …`) and does not fail the test. The fixture reads the sign-in link the server prints. The `page` fixture opens the link of a new code, so it starts signed in, with the tour done, the sandboxes' setup answered (New chat asks nothing, whatever the computer lacks) and the UI in English; `test.use()` changes that (`lang: 'zh-TW'`, `signIn: false`, `tour: true`, `sandboxAsk: true`), adds environment for the server (`serverEnv`), leaves CLIs not installed (`missingClis`), starts the server without the fake endpoint as a model backend, so that the open agents are installed but not ready (`openBackend: false`), or with OpenHands' terminal switch off (`sandboxTerminal: false`); `live: true` runs the real CLIs instead (the [live tests](#live-tests)). `relay.api()` calls the API as a signed-in browser, to set things up or check what was stored, and `relay.fakeLog()` returns what the fake CLIs were started with and given. A failed test gets the server's output and the fake CLIs' log attached, besides its trace, screenshot and video.
- **The fake CLIs** ([`e2e/fake-cli/`](../e2e/fake-cli/), CommonJS using only Node's built-ins) stand in for all twelve CLIs and speak each one's protocol as its adapter drives it: Claude Code's stream-json with its control requests and `can_use_tool`, `codex app-server`, ACP for Grok, Cursor (`cursor-agent acp`), Antigravity (`agy acp`), Copilot (`copilot --acp`, answering `/sandbox policy` without the model, as Copilot does), Muse Code (`muse acp`), OpenCode, Goose, Cline and OpenHands, and Pi's JSONL RPC (`pi --mode rpc`, with Harness Relay's real gate extension, `piGate.js`, loaded as Pi loads it); and they answer the probes the server runs (`--version`, the sign-in status, the models, `grok inspect --json`, what each CLI loads by itself), exiting with an error at once on anything else. Each CLI's fake is a module of its own (`agents/<provider>.cjs`, with its file name, label, version, home and API key check), beside what they share (`lib/`: JSON-RPC and ACP on stdio, a turn's steps, homes, files and the log), and `fake-cli.cjs` picks the module by the name the process runs under (`cursor-agent` is Cursor, `agy` Antigravity). `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts` bundles the modules into one script when it installs them (`bundleFakeCli()`, a small module loader of its own): a Node single executable's `require` loads only Node's built-in modules, and as a script the fake runs from the cache folder, where the modules are not. What the specs need to know of each CLI and its fake (its tools' names, what it asks about, how it takes a refusal, where its home is, and whether its sign-in is asked in an account's own folder: `statusInOwnFolder`, false for Copilot, which is asked in a home of the question's own with a copy of the account's sign-in state) is in `e2e/support/clis/<provider>.ts`, changed together with the fake, and `e2e/support/clis.ts` answers the specs' questions with it (`eachCli()`, the two teams of `HALVES`, `basePermission()`, `needsBelowFull()`, `defaultHome()`, `sessionFile()`). The server finds the fakes first on its PATH, by name, as it finds the real ones. On macOS and Linux each one is the bundled script with a `#!` line for the Node that runs the tests (OpenHands' in a Python virtual environment of its own, as `uv tool install` lays it out, where the server reads its models from LiteLLM's list). Windows starts only real programs (`.exe`) without a shell, so there `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts` makes the script a Node single executable application (`node --experimental-sea-config`, then `postject`), linked under the twelve names and as `gh` (below). That takes a few seconds and about 100 MB, so it is kept in `node_modules/.cache/harness-relay-e2e/` until a module or Node changes (and then for an hour after a server last started with it, for another run that may still be using it; a server refuses to start when one of its fakes is missing, rather than find the real CLIs). `E2E_FAKE_SEA=1` builds them that way on macOS and Linux too. On Windows postject warns `The signature seems corrupted!`: injecting the script breaks `node.exe`'s Authenticode signature, which the copy does not need. For `e2e/specs/windows.spec.ts`, `installFakeLaunchers()` also makes the fakes as some Windows users' CLIs come: Cursor's own launcher (`cursor-agent.cmd`, a Node and a script of Cursor's) and npm's `.cmd` shims, for a Node script (Cline, Pi) and for a program (OpenCode). [`test/e2e-fakes.test.ts`](../test/e2e-fakes.test.ts) bundles and runs the fakes with `npm test`, so a module that does not load shows before an end-to-end run.
- **What the fakes answer** depends on the prompt, as a model's would. The lead agent's prompts get a team (`e2e:team=claude,cursor,pi`, by provider id, `e2e:mode=relay`, `e2e:rounds=2`, `e2e:name=Some_name`, `e2e:template=<id>`, `e2e:badplan` for an answer that is not JSON, `e2e:slowplan` for one that takes about ten seconds (`e2e:slowplan=<seconds>` for another time); one Claude member by default; in a review, `e2e:reviewquote=list` quotes across the two items of the list `e2e:markdown` writes, and `e2e:reviewquote=none` quotes nothing), a group (the first group that the first message names), a changed team (`e2e:add=<cli>` adds a member, `e2e:slowrevise=<seconds>` takes that long) and a review of the team's replies (one highlight quoting the first words of the first reply, a risk unless `e2e:reviewkind=<kind>` says otherwise, a step for the user and one for the team; `e2e:badreview` for an answer that is not JSON, `e2e:slowreview` for one that takes about ten seconds, read from the user's messages it reviews), and a tidying of the long-term memory (it pins the first note in use an agent wrote that is not pinned yet, and says how many notes it read: `tidyReply`), and an approval request it reviews ("Approve for me": approved, unless the user's latest message says `e2e:flag`; `e2e:slowapprove=<seconds>` for a review that takes that long). A turn gets `Hello from <name> (<CLI>). You said: “<the user's latest message>”.`, with the images and the tool library's MCP servers it got, streamed in pieces and changed by directives in that message: `e2e:think`, `e2e:tool` (a listing of the working directory with the CLI's own tool), `e2e:todo`, `e2e:approve` (a command that needs approval; `e2e:approve=publish`, `npm publish e2e-approval`, which asks in edit mode too, for Pi and for Copilot, which runs `echo` without asking), `e2e:fetch=<url>` (reaches the URL: a host the run may not reach without asking), `e2e:write=<path>` and `e2e:writeother=<path>` (in the first other working directory the CLI was given), `e2e:ask`, `e2e:steps` (ends the reply with a `<steps>` block of three steps for the user, the first with a link), `e2e:handoff=<Name>`, `e2e:slow` (about ten seconds, to be stopped; `e2e:slow=<seconds>` for a test that waits for its end; a reply of several paragraphs one after another), `e2e:fail` (exits with an error), `e2e:error` (fails inside the protocol), `e2e:flaky` (fails once, answers the retry; for Cursor, as a chat's first message), `e2e:long` (forty more paragraphs, a reply taller than the window), `e2e:markdown`, `e2e:mermaid` (a Mermaid diagram), `e2e:widecode` (a code block of one line far wider than a phone, which scrolls sideways), `e2e:memory` (says the tool library's memory notes its briefing gave it), `e2e:env=<NAME>,<OTHER>` (says whether each variable is set in its environment, never its value), `e2e:remember` (ends the reply with a `<remember>` block saving what the user said, without the directives), `e2e:secret` (ends it with a `<secret>` block, `E2E_TOKEN` unless `=<NAME>`), `e2e:command` (a `<command>` block proposing `e2e-check`, which prints its secrets file and the input it read), `e2e:commandrun` (a `<command-run>` block, one run of it in the outbox), `e2e:publishes` (with `e2e:command`, the command marked `publishes="yes"`), `e2e:propose` (a `<schedule>` block; `=mail` one that waits for a new mail, `=command` one every hour that only runs `e2e-check`), `e2e:secreturl=<url>` (a `<secret>` block naming that page, the card's link), `e2e:interactive` (an `<interactive>` block whose command prints a link and a code and asks for a password; `=askpass` asks through the password helper instead), `e2e:recall` (says the long-term memory notes its turn was given: those in its briefing's `<memory_notes>` and its prompt's `<recalled_memory>`) and `e2e:skills` (reads each tool-library skill its briefing names, where the CLI may read, and says its first line) (`DIRECTIVES` in `e2e/fake-cli/lib/plan.cjs`), and for Cursor alone `e2e:delete=<path>` (a delete, which Cursor always asks about). `@<cli>` after a directive keeps it to that CLI, by provider id: `e2e:write=a.txt@cursor`. A later round with nothing new from the user is `[PASS]`. Like a model that ignores its briefing, a fake always tries what a directive asks, and whether it happens depends only on what Harness Relay gave the CLI: Claude Code's `--permission-mode`, `--disallowedTools` and `--add-dir` folders, Codex's sandbox or permission profile (with its `write` entries; `writable_roots`) and approval policy, Grok's yolo mode and the `read_write` folders of its sandbox profile (without a sandbox, in yolo mode and on Windows, the other working directories its briefing names), Cursor's `--force`, approval mode and the allow and deny rules of its config folder, Antigravity's settings, mode and flags, Copilot's flags (`--sandbox`, `--allow-all`, `--add-dir`, `--excluded-tools skill`), the settings of its run's home (its sandbox's policy) and the session's `allow_all`, Muse Code's permission profile (in its config folder's settings), session mode and flags, each open agent's config, environment and the files its adapter writes, and the answers to each one's approval requests (a decline's note reaches the CLIs that take one). Cursor and Antigravity have no sandbox, so their fakes ask as the real ones do; Copilot's fake works out what its sandbox's policy allows, as the real one keeps to it (a write outside it fails without asking); the open agents' fakes write and reach URLs for real, so below full access what is not theirs is refused by Harness Relay's own sandbox itself, and they report the refusal as their CLI does. So a test that writes in a read-only chat or in another folder tells whether the adapter set the CLI up right. The fakes keep their sessions in their homes (an open agent's in its member's state folder) and refuse to resume one they never started, as the real CLIs do. They find skills of their own where the real CLIs look (Claude Code: its home's `skills/` and each working directory's `.claude/skills/`; Codex: its home's `skills/`; Grok: the project's `.claude/skills/`, only in a folder it trusts; Copilot: a project's `.github/skills/` and its home's `skills/`) and honour what Harness Relay leaves out (`skillOverrides`, `skills.config`, `GROK_CLAUDE_SKILLS_ENABLED`, Copilot's `--excluded-tools skill`). They are signed in until their own sign-out (`auth logout`, `logout`, Antigravity's and Copilot's over ACP) in their home, and `claude auth login` prints a link and waits for a code (Cursor's `login` finishes after `E2E_FAKE_CURSOR_LOGIN_MS`, or a line on its stdin; Cursor's sign-in is one per computer user, as the real one's, and a key signs a run in by itself). Antigravity's fake keeps its sign-in too, but Harness Relay starts it only with a `GEMINI_API_KEY`, which each test server gives the default account (`DEFAULT_KEY` in `support/relay.ts`, for the CLIs whose facts say `defaultKeyOnly`); a spec that needs it signed out sets that variable empty. `relay.fakeLog()` also has each start's home and whether it got an API key, and the one the test expects (`E2E_EXPECTED_API_KEY`; never the key), each turn's model, effort and skills (and Grok's trust), and the values the tool library's MCP servers got; each line names the process that wrote it (`proc`): match a process's lines by that, not by its pid, which Windows soon gives to a new process. `E2E_FAKE_SIGNED_OUT=codex,cursor` (through `serverEnv`, by provider id) signs those CLIs out (`gh` too). `E2E_FAKE_CURSOR_TEAM_ALLOW` gives the fake Cursor a team's auto-run allowlist (command prefixes it then runs without asking).
- **The fake model endpoint** (`FakeEndpoint` in `e2e/support/relay.ts`): each test server has a small HTTP server of its own beside it, set up as the model backend `e2e` (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>/v1`, its models `e2e-model` and `e2e-model-mini`), with `e2e/e2e-model` each open agent's default model, so the open agents are ready in every test that installs them. No model answers there: it lists its models (`GET /v1/models`), and it takes the log lines of the fakes that run inside Harness Relay's own sandbox (`POST /e2e-log`, `E2E_FAKE_LOG_URL`), which may not write the log file (`E2E_FAKE_LOG`) there: the run's backend is the one host the sandbox lets a run reach, and `relay.ts` adds those lines to the same file, a moment after the reply (read them once the turn has `settled()`, or with `logged()` from `e2e/support/clis.ts`). The open agents run below full access in the real sandbox on macOS, and on Linux with bubblewrap, socat and ripgrep: `e2e/global-setup.ts` works out once whether it can work here (`E2E_RELAY_SANDBOX`: `ok`, or why not; `e2e/support/sandbox.ts`), and a test that needs it is skipped where it cannot, saying why, or fails instead with `E2E_REQUIRE_RELAY_SANDBOX=1` (as on CI). Where a CLI runs only with full access (the open agents on Windows, OpenCode on Linux), a test about permissions is skipped for it (`needsBelowFull()`), and any other runs it with full access (`basePermission()`); a test of every CLI runs as two teams, the seven with a sign-in and the five open agents, since a team has at most eight members (and one the lead agent designs by itself at most five, so `chat.spec.ts` asks for the seven in teams of four and three).
- **The fake folder dialog** ([`e2e/fake-cli/fake-dialog.cjs`](../e2e/fake-cli/fake-dialog.cjs)) is installed with them as `zenity` on macOS and Linux. Linux looks for its dialog on the PATH in a graphical session, so a test that sets `DISPLAY` (through `serverEnv`) gets the box's folder button, and the fake answers with the folders `E2E_FAKE_PICK` lists (JSON, each resolved from the folder it opens at), or is cancelled when it lists none. Its arguments and character set go to `relay.fakeLog()` (`cli: 'zenity'`). macOS and Windows open their own dialogs by their full paths, so those tests run on Linux only.
- **The fake npm** ([`e2e/fake-cli/fake-npm.cjs`](../e2e/fake-cli/fake-npm.cjs)) is installed with them as `npm` on every system (on Windows, the same executable under that name, as for `gh`), for installing a CLI from Settings. It answers `--version` and `install --global --prefix <folder> <package>`: a CLI's fake put in that folder as npm's global layout would (on macOS and Linux a link `bin/<command>` to the fake; on Windows the fake as `node_modules\<package>\bin\<command>.exe` with npm's shim for it, `<command>.cmd`), held while the test's `fake-npm/hold` is there, failing as npm does for a package it cannot find on `fake-npm/fail`; it logs its arguments (`cli: 'npm'`) and refuses everything else, so nothing reaches a registry.
- **The fake GitHub CLI** ([`e2e/fake-cli/fake-gh.cjs`](../e2e/fake-cli/fake-gh.cjs)) is installed with them as `gh` on every system (on Windows, the fake CLIs' executable under that name: it runs the fake GitHub CLI when started as `gh`, so one build serves all twelve names). It answers what sharing and a problem report's screenshots ask of `gh` (`--version`, `gist create`, `gist delete`, `api gists/<id>`), and the share dialog's sign-in (`auth login --web --hostname github.com`: its code and GitHub's device page, then signed in once the test writes `fake-github/approve`, kept as `fake-github/signed-in`, or failing on `fake-github/deny`), keeps its gists in the test's folder (`fake-github/`), logs its arguments (`cli: 'gh'`) and refuses everything else, so nothing reaches GitHub. AgentsView needs no fake program: `e2e/specs/sessions.spec.ts` starts a small HTTP server on 127.0.0.1 for it.
- **Before the tests**, [`e2e/global-setup.ts`](../e2e/global-setup.ts) builds the UI when a file it is built from is newer than the build, both as `npm run build` makes it (`dist/web`) and as a development build (`dist/web-dev`, `NODE_ENV=development`: React's StrictMode runs each effect twice, as under `npm run dev`) for the specs that serve it (`RELAY_WEB_DIR` set to `DEV_WEB_DIR` from `e2e/support/paths.ts`). `E2E_BUILD=always` builds both every run, `E2E_BUILD=never` never does (CI builds them in steps of their own, the same way). Then it installs the fakes, and works out once whether Harness Relay's own sandbox can work on this computer (`E2E_RELAY_SANDBOX`, for the workers).
- **Writing a test**: in `e2e/specs/<area>.spec.ts`, import `test` and `expect` from `../fixtures`, and the steps from [`e2e/support/app.ts`](../e2e/support/app.ts) (`startChat`, which unticks lead review unless the test asks for it with `leadReview: true`, so that other specs see no reviews (a spec that starts a chat on the New chat page by itself unticks it too), `send`, `confirmTeam`, `finishedReply`, `openTeamChat` for a chat with a team of its own, made through the API, `countPages` for the pages a tab loads, reloads included, and so on). For a test of each CLI, use the helpers of [`e2e/support/clis.ts`](../e2e/support/clis.ts) (`eachCli(CLIS, …)`, `chatWith()`, `eachHalf()` with `teamOf()`, `needsBelowFull()`), and take what differs from one CLI to another from its facts (`e2e/support/clis/<provider>.ts`), not from its name. A test that uses a CLI besides Claude Code, Codex and Grok outside those helpers installs it with `withClis()` in its `test.describe` (`withEveryCli()` for a test of them all); each one more installed makes every page of the test slower to open. With `E2E_SYSTEM_CLIS=1` (CI sets it) a CLI's own tests run only on the systems its facts name (`testedOn`: Linux always, and macOS or Windows where Harness Relay runs it differently there in a way its fake can tell, its file saying why), and are skipped elsewhere, saying where they run; without it, they run everywhere. A signed-in page starts on the home page (`homePage`); `openNewChat` opens the New chat page from the sidebar. Find elements by their role and visible text where the UI gives them (English unless the test changes the language), CSS classes only otherwise. Something new an agent should do goes in the fakes as a new directive (`DIRECTIVES` in `e2e/fake-cli/lib/plan.cjs`), which each CLI's module then plays in its own protocol.

Current specs (each scenario in [`e2e/README.md`](../e2e/README.md)):

- `e2e/specs/home.spec.ts` — the home page and its links to a new chat and a waiting team, requiring a nonblank question, keeping its attachments and workspace through lead settings, sending it once, the sidebar's link home, and the logo's animation: once, `transform` and `opacity` alone, none with reduced motion.
- `e2e/specs/chat.spec.ts` — a new chat: the lead agent's team card, confirming it and the reply; teams used without asking; a team of every CLI answering in order (the seven with a sign-in, and the five open agents; a half with more CLIs than the lead agent puts in a new team, five, answers as teams of about the same size, each in a chat of its own, so the seven with a sign-in as teams of four and three); a follow-up question with a team of its own.
- `e2e/specs/activity.spec.ts` — each CLI's thinking, tool call (its own listing tool, with its output) and to-do list (none for Cline and Pi); a reply in Markdown (heading, list, table, code).
- `e2e/specs/approvals.spec.ts` — each CLI asking before a command (Pi and Copilot in edit mode, about `npm publish`): allowed (it runs), declined with a note (shown on the card; passed on to Claude Code, Grok, OpenCode, Goose and Pi, dropped by Codex, Cursor, Muse Code, Cline and OpenHands, and Antigravity and Copilot ending their turn); "Always allow" answering the next request by itself; stopping while a request waits; each CLI with full access running and writing anywhere without asking; a chat that refuses automatically.
- `e2e/specs/files.spec.ts` — edit mode: a file written, `git init`, the changes card and the turn's diff (a folded section for the file, opened and folded again); each CLI in a read-only chat writing nothing (Claude Code and Pi without their file tools, Cursor refused by its own rules, the others asking and declined), and Grok's reason when refused automatically; each CLI's turn recording its own change, in two teams; parallel mode's worktrees and applying one result; the file browser and a file's preview; a folder's ZIP download (its files, no `.git`) and, for one too large, the offer without what git ignores; editing a text file in Files (CRLF kept, a save over a newer version refused with Reload, unsaved changes asking before closing), a new file and uploads by the picker and by a drop (a file there replaced only once confirmed), and a save refused while a run writes in the folder.
- `e2e/specs/folders.spec.ts` — in edit mode, a folder the chat was not given staying out of each CLI's reach when refused automatically (the open agents' writes refused by Harness Relay's own sandbox, Copilot's by its own without asking), and Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Antigravity writing there once the user allows it; several working directories: folders added and removed in the box, each CLI writing in each folder with the changes card listing them by folder and the diff a folded section for each file (all opened and folded at once) (Grok on Windows told them by its briefing alone, Cursor asking before the second), the file browser's folders, parallel mode's worktree of each folder applied together, and Settings' default working directories. On Linux, the folder dialog (the fake zenity): the folders chosen in it added to the box and the chat starting in them, the dialog opened inside the last folder with several allowed, and in Chinese on a server without a UTF-8 locale its Chinese title and `LC_CTYPE=C.UTF-8`, with Cancel adding nothing.
- `e2e/specs/turns.spec.ts` — asking the user and answering, skipping a question, relay hand-offs, an @mention answered by that member only, passing in a later round, each CLI's reply stopped, its error (an exit, and one inside its protocol) and Retry, its next turn resuming its own session, a member whose CLI lost its session starting again with the chat so far, a message sent mid-reply reaching the next member.
- `e2e/specs/scroll.spec.ts` — scrolling the chat: scrolled up, the arrow in the middle above the message box (centred on it, over the messages, nothing over it) taking the chat to its end by click (the message box keeping its focus and text, or staying unfocused), by Enter (focus going on to the message box), with reduced motion at once, and gone at the end; while a slow long reply streams, a reader who scrolled up staying in place with the arrow, which takes the chat to the end and follows the rest of the reply; the arrow above a message waiting in the tray, and hidden while the @mention list is open.
- `e2e/specs/history.spec.ts` — a message edited and sent again (what came after it replaced), Retry on a finished reply, a conversation branched from a reply.
- `e2e/specs/team.spec.ts` — the lead agent adding a member when asked in Team settings, and the new member answering once saved; a template standing in when the lead agent's answer cannot be read; a team proposed for a new question declined, and the current team answering; the add card as tall as the members beside it (its list a named group that scrolls, with a shadow while more are below), listing every CLI on a row of its own, and one tall card stretching only its row.
- `e2e/specs/steps.spec.ts` — steps cards: a member's steps (`e2e:steps`) shown as a card, the team waiting, ticked with the keyboard and kept for a reload and another tab, answered with Done (with a note, steps left unticked reported as not done) and the member that asked going on first, then the card answered; "I'm stuck" at a step with a note; a free-text answer instead leaving the card as it was; in parallel mode with another member's question waiting, the card saying so and its answer going to its own member alone, the question still waiting for its own; on a phone, the card fitting the screen and ticked and answered by touch.
- `e2e/specs/handoffs.spec.ts` — secrets and fixed commands: a secret a reply asks for (`e2e:secret`) taken out of its text into a card, masked until shown, saved, the team told its name, the value nowhere on the page nor in the chat the server sends, listed by name in Settings → Secrets and commands and removed there; a proposed fixed command (`e2e:command`) saved, tried in Harness Relay's own sandbox with its secret masked in its output, the result sent to the team, and one run of it from the outbox (`e2e:commandrun`) sent with its input (where that sandbox runs: not on Windows).
- `e2e/specs/interactive.spec.ts` — commands the user runs: an `<interactive>` card (`e2e:interactive`) started from its card, its link and code picked out, typed into hidden, its output masked and sent to the team, and the password helper answered on the card (`e2e:interactive=askpass`); not on Windows.
- `e2e/specs/handover.spec.ts` — the chat's browser handed to the user (`browser_hand_over` through `e2e:mcp`): the screencast with a mouse and keys and a note, declined, on a phone in Chinese; and its window, headless (`RELAY_HEADLESS_WINDOWS`), handed back with the page opened again without one.
- `e2e/specs/memory.spec.ts` — long-term memory: a note each CLI saves (`e2e:remember`) taken out of its reply's text into a card, kept once, and given to a later chat's agents (`e2e:recall`); the card setting a note aside and restoring it; Settings listing who wrote each note and how it is known, pinning the user's own, and Tidy up now with the fake lead agent (it pins an agent's note: `tidyReply`), the pinned notes then in every chat; memory off until turned on in Labs (no note saved, no card, the Tool library without it, Labs showing only its switch until it is on).
- `e2e/specs/review.spec.ts` — lead review: the review card (summary, the point with its kind, quote and note, the steps), the words it quotes marked in the reply (CSS Custom Highlight API) with the reply's tag and links between them, a team step put in the message box, and once sent its own review marking the new reply while the first keeps its one mark; every new chat starting with it on (the New chat page ticks the box again after a chat started without it); off unless asked (a chat made through the API), turned on in Team settings and reading the latest question; Stop, and an answer that is no review; a message sent during a review (the message box's hint and placeholder, Enter stopping the review and sending at once, the queue tray never shown, the next review reading from where the stopped one started) and Team settings saving the box alone while it runs, which stops that review, with no "Stopped." note, and both stopped cards then saying lead review is off now; a schedule's message that comes due during a review (sent with `POST …/schedule/run`) waiting in the tray (**1 waiting**, its note), and "Skip the review and send now" stopping the review and sending it, the team answering it; the design card's box, which the lead agent leaves alone (its prompt says nothing of it): ticked there, the question being answered and the next one (with a design of its own, its box still ticked, the first card's box gone) reviewed, and unticked, the next answer not; the New automation page's box, ticked at first, giving the schedule's chat (one that opens a new chat each time) the setting as set there, ticked or not, which its Team settings show; a shared page keeping the card without the app's buttons; a quote marked across list items, in its kind's colour (a decision); the box going back when the server refuses; and the card in Chinese on a 320px phone.
- `e2e/specs/inputs.spec.ts` — what reaches each CLI besides the prompt: an MCP server from the tool library (its secret kept off the command line), one over HTTP (its secret header too; not Antigravity, whose adapter gives it none), and an attached image (not Cline, which takes none).
- `e2e/specs/sandbox.spec.ts` — Harness Relay's own sandbox around the open agents: in a read-only chat a write the user allows still refused, and a host the run may not reach asked about and refused when declined; an open agent below full access where it runs only with full access (Windows; OpenCode on Linux) not started, saying why; OpenHands on macOS without its terminal switch; Antigravity asking about a project's own setup before agy starts, and not starting when refused; Copilot asking about a project's hooks before it starts, not starting when declined, and once allowed running the turn.
- `e2e/specs/sidebar.spec.ts` — a group and the lead agent sorting a new conversation into it, renaming and deleting (the home page opening in place of the deleted chat), search by what an agent wrote, a title given at the start; dragging a conversation into a group and out, and a branched family moving as one; the pin over a conversation's top-left corner (shown on hover, tilted; once pinned, always shown, upright and yellow), the Pinned section above the groups (the one pinned last first, each still in its group, kept after a reload, collapsed and remembered), the pin clear of the title with its icon centred, pinning from the keyboard, a pinned row's folder menu, a failed pin, unpinning from either place and deleting a pinned conversation; every CLI's health dot and the count on one line, the chevron's tip at the report icon's edge; the star left of the report icon: its dialog, its picture's press (played three times; not with reduced motion) and its link to the repository in a new tab.
- `e2e/specs/models.spec.ts` — a member's model and effort reaching each CLI (Claude Code's flags, Codex's thread, Grok's session options, the others as their adapters pass them; an open agent's model from the fake endpoint), and Settings' default model and effort for a member left on Default.
- `e2e/specs/oauth.spec.ts` covers shared client settings, service-specific Google scopes, retries, bilingual setup guides and the disabled actions for an incomplete registration. It also exercises an account signing in with Microsoft (a stand-in for its sign-in page and Graph, `RELAY_MICROSOFT_LOGIN_URL` and `RELAY_MICROSOFT_GRAPH_URL`), coming back to the web app's own /oauth/callback, its calendars joining the calendar tools (its own listed as added), its sign-in revoked (a calendar's **Test** then says so, and the account shows **Needs a new sign-in** and **Sign in again**), signed in again and revoked once more (its calendars' list then finds it, and the account says so again), and removed; and one signing in for mail alone (stand-ins for Exchange Online's IMAP and SMTP, `RELAY_MICROSOFT_IMAP` and `RELAY_MICROSOFT_SMTP`): its mail account added with no password nor servers to edit, its **Test** signing in to both over XOAUTH2 with Exchange Online's token, removed and added again with **Add mailbox**, and removed with its account.
- `e2e/specs/app-publish.spec.ts` publishes a static preview against stand-ins for Render's and Neon's APIs (`e2e/support/cloud.ts`, `RELAY_RENDER_API_URL`, `RELAY_NEON_API_URL`) and the fake `gh`, which makes each private repository as a bare one where the server's git pushes (`RELAY_PUBLISH_GIT_DIR`): the keys saved in Settings (a wrong one refused, the workspace and organization shown, the key never), **Publish…** in the panel, the dialog's plan, **Publish** only once confirmed as public, the steps until live with its address, Render's static site made from the repository holding the page alone, **Publish update** with a second commit on the same site, and **Unpublish** from Settings with the repository kept; and the dialog naming what Settings still needs, and refusing files changed since the preview.
- `e2e/specs/teams.spec.ts` checks installation status across reload and retry. An offline PowerShell stand-in exercises module installation, read and posting device consent, assistant selection, a real gateway call and per-message outbox confirmation.
- `e2e/specs/mcp-oauth.spec.ts` — a tool library MCP server's own sign-in (a stand-in MCP server that answers 401 and its authorization server): **Sign in** opening a tab on the click, registering Harness Relay with the page's own redirect address and sending the tab to the authorization page, back to /oauth/callback, the server shown signed in, a fake Claude Code's turn given its access token in the server's `Authorization` header; revoked there, the next reply's note that the server was left out, **Needs a new sign-in** and **Sign in again** (no new registration, the replaced refresh token revoked), and **Sign out** revoking its token; and a server whose sign-in page is on another host saying where first, **Cancel** opening nothing, **Continue** opening it and signing in.
- `e2e/specs/tools.spec.ts` — the tool library's page, with a member of each CLI, in two teams: an MCP server (its secret masked and off the command line), a skill picked from a folder and read by each CLI, memory for all and for one CLI, each turned off for a CLI or in a member's Tools; an MCP server given a new value and removed, and a skill and a memory note removed. Also what a member's Tools say of its own CLI's setup: nothing while the library loads, then with the library empty, with nothing for its CLI, and over its items. Also a member reaching Harness Relay's own tools through its MCP gateway, and a mail it sends waiting in the outbox until the user's Send (sent once), or discarded. Also, in full access, an open agent (OpenCode's fake, from its session's `mcpServers`) reaching them too, its mail waiting in the outbox. Also, below full access, the CLI's own question about Harness Relay's tool answered for the user (no card for it), and the gateway's card about an act (adding a calendar event) saying what it will do. Also the browser to start set in Settings: a path with no file there refused with why, a file saved, shown in use and kept, and emptied again.
- `e2e/specs/app-lifecycle.spec.ts` connects local fixture accounts for Mail, Calendar, GitHub, Obsidian, Notion and Heptabase, and configures Chromium. It enables each tool, checks a real provider result in the fake Claude Code chat, then disables access and checks the next turn. The Google Docs and Slack cases in `app-confirmation.spec.ts` do the same after a confirmed write.
- `e2e/specs/app-confirmation.spec.ts` checks outbox confirmation for Google Docs creation and text edits, writing a note to the selected Obsidian vault and posting to Slack in full access. It checks read-only Drive access, adding a Docs grant, and pausing and resuming an authorized Google account. It also checks the Mail and Calendar service choosers, the Gmail OAuth action and the iCloud Mail app-specific password, the Apple Calendar (iCloud) connection flow, settings form labels, and cancelling or confirming sign-out from the shared GitHub CLI account before signing back in.
- `e2e/specs/share.spec.ts` — copying a reply's Markdown and the conversation (Chromium); sharing as a secret gist through the fake `gh`, listed in Settings and removed; `gh` signed out (signed in from the dialog with GitHub's code, then the link made once, also on a slow network; a sign-in still waiting picked up when the dialog opens again, and kept through a failed request; cancelled and started again; refused by GitHub; signed in from Settings' removal), and missing (not on Windows).
- `e2e/specs/accounts.spec.ts` — for each CLI with a sign-in: an added account's own home (Cursor's, Antigravity's and Muse Code's signed in with an API key; Copilot's sign-in asked in a home of the question's own; Antigravity's default account's API key box open from the start), signing in and out per account (Claude Code's sign-in flow; Codex, Grok and Copilot signed out on their own), an account's API key with the credential store off (Claude Code, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code: masked, given to its CLI only; Codex: through `codex login --with-api-key` in its folder), the server's own key for the default account only (Antigravity's default account running on the server's key, which the test servers give it), removing an account (signed out, its folder deleted, its member and the lead agent on the default account), and no removal while a conversation runs; an account added while an older status answer is still on its way keeping the newer answer's state (that answer held back with `page.route`), and a CLI signed out outside Harness Relay shown so when Settings is opened again within the server's cache time (the app's cached answer held until Settings' own has landed).
- `e2e/specs/native.spec.ts` — what each CLI loads by itself: its own skill listed and left out of the next run (Claude Code, Codex), and Grok's trust in a folder (from the Grok card, and from a new chat's team card, trusted or declined).
- `e2e/specs/sessions.spec.ts` — the CLIs' own folder, in two teams: sessions kept there, a fresh session after it changes, folders refused, no change while a conversation runs; AgentsView (a stand-in server): a conversation and its members' sessions sent, a Cursor member's recorded session sent after the thread and linked under it, Copilot's and Muse Code's own sessions linked under it as `copilot:<id>` and `muse:<id>` with none recorded for them, an address elsewhere refused, a failed update shown in Settings.
- `e2e/specs/report.spec.ts` — a problem report: the mail's `mailto:` and Gmail links with the details, a draft kept, a report too long for the link, screenshots to upload first, and screenshots that cannot be uploaded (`gh` signed out), or uploaded again by themselves once gh is signed in from the dialog.
- `e2e/specs/appearance.spec.ts` — the guided tour (topics checked as they are shown, in any order; the home page's question about the topics left, Show me and Not now; a browser that finished an older tour asked about the topics changed; a topic's button; on a phone a sheet at the bottom showing a topic or the list, whose long topic scrolls above its buttons; on a wide screen one size and Next in one place; each topic's picture) and the theme (dark or light kept, following the system).
- `e2e/specs/notifications.spec.ts` — Settings → Notifications and sound (the browser's permission asked, the sound tried, a test notification), and what the page does when the team finishes or needs the user, recorded by stand-ins, without push: a team to confirm, a reply and a question each shown with the window behind another, a click opening the chat, nothing for the chat in front and only the sound for another one; the notification inbox: the bell's unread count with notifications and sound off (and no system notification), its list with each chat's title, a notification opening its chat and reading it, the chat on screen reading its own at once, Mark all as read and Clear all, and the bell right of the expand button, which keeps the corner, with the sidebar collapsed.
- `e2e/specs/templates.spec.ts` — the built-in templates and their members, the lead agent using one as it is, saving a designed team as a template; in a template's editor three columns wide, the add card on a row of its own and beside members; a new template's members on the installed CLIs only, also when opened before their statuses came, and a template's member on a CLI not installed moved to one that is, said so, and saveable.
- `e2e/specs/limits.spec.ts` — a server started with `RELAY_MAX_TEAM_AGENTS`, `RELAY_MAX_ACCOUNTS` and `RELAY_TURN_TIMEOUT_MIN`: `GET /api/limits`, the team editor adding no more members (and a larger template refused), Settings adding no more accounts, a built-in template (three members) saying why it cannot be used and the lead agent falling back to the general one cut to two, and a schedule's hint giving the timeout.
- `e2e/specs/schedule.spec.ts` — a weekly automation and an event draft through lead and connector settings, the optional goal before the trigger, and a chat’s schedule saved and run once; an automation that starts when new mail comes, against a stand-in IMAP server (the first Check now notes the mail there, and a matching mail that arrives reaches the team); one that starts when a file appears in the working directory (a new report reaches the team by its path, never its contents); one that starts when a pull request opens on GitHub, through the fake gh (the form refuses what is not a repository, the first Check now notes those there, and a new one reaches the team as data its title cannot close); one that waits for two file patterns (**Add another** with an empty pattern, the same pattern twice refused, each one's state on a line of its own after Check now, a file matching either reaching the team in a message of its own, only paused once it is off, and **Remove** leaving the other one).
- `e2e/specs/settings.spec.ts` — the Settings overview’s links for missing CLIs and open agents without a backend, the sidebar's health dots on one line (how many are ready, their names shown and kept after a reload); each CLI's version and sign-in (the open agents "Ready" on the fake endpoint), an executable path (a wrong one shows the CLI missing), the language kept after a reload, another CLI chosen as the lead agent; each CLI with a sign-in when it is not signed in (Settings, the sidebar, and its reply's error; Antigravity without a Gemini API key: no **Sign in**, its key-only note and its API key box open, and agy never started but for its version); Cursor and Antigravity not installed (`missingClis`); an open agent without a model backend not ready, and its turn's error (`openBackend: false`); the links row's Agents menu: its items and group, its keys (Tab and Shift+Tab out of it), a click on its heading, the focus or a click elsewhere, choosing an agent, the button naming it at one width, an agent's address; in a short window inside it, the keys scrolling it to the item they reach; on a phone the button whole once the row narrows, Tab out bringing the next link into view, the menu inside the screen and following its button as the row scrolls; and following its button as the row sticks beside the collapsed sidebar's button.
- `e2e/specs/windows.spec.ts` — on Windows only, the CLIs started through the launchers they come with there (Cursor's own `cursor-agent.cmd`, npm's shims for a Node script and for a program), set as their executable path in Settings: each found with its version, and answering.
- `e2e/specs/signin.spec.ts` — the sign-in page, a wrong code, signing in with a code, a one-time sign-in link (a used-up one shows its error), a link opened in a tab that shows the sign-in page already, a link's error gone after signing in with a code and being signed out, a link opened in a tab signed in already that keeps it signed in while requests sent with the old key are still on their way (their answers, "signed out", reach the page together with the sign-in's; never a sign-out), a tab showing the sign-in page following a sign-in in another tab of the browser, a link opened in another tab keeping every tab signed in while the first has requests on their way, a tab still on its first check following another tab's sign-in (its first answer out of date by then), a sign-in that gets no answer given up after 30 seconds with a message (on the page's clock), signing out in Settings; in a development build of the UI (React's StrictMode), a link opening the app on the first load (asking once whether the browser is signed in), a wrong one showing its error, the same link in a tab signed in already, and a link opened in another tab.
- `e2e/specs/install.spec.ts` — installing a CLI from its card in Settings with the fake npm: Copilot not found, installed into its own folder (under way with the installer's output, then its version and the program as its executable, the sidebar's dot no longer red, npm given a global install into that folder), and removed (the folder gone, not installed again); Cline's install failing (npm's words and output, nothing left), tried again and cancelled; Grok's card offering its maker's bash script on macOS and Linux and its PowerShell installer on Windows.
- `e2e/specs/sandbox-setup.spec.ts` — the dialog New chat opens while the sandboxes lack something (the server's answers the test's own): strongly recommended, Not now leaving it to Settings → Sandboxes and not asking again, an install from it with its outcome, what cannot be installed with its command and no Install (Escape closing it for the page only), nothing asked when nothing is missing, and the same in a development build (StrictMode).
- `e2e/specs/voice.spec.ts` — voice input's install in Settings (its status from GET /api/voice with an install put in, since a real one downloads): its steps in line, their state for screen readers (the current one `aria-current`), the download's percent and its bar's fill; a failed install's stopped step. It also checks the microphone badge in the two creation forms and the chat composer, the positions of the creation forms’ file and voice buttons during use, and the release of the audio track when microphone access is granted after navigation.
- `e2e/specs/i18n.spec.ts` — the UI (the home page's tagline too) and the lead agent's card in Traditional Chinese with the prompts in English; the sign-in page and the server's own messages in Chinese. In the desktop app's window (Electron in the user agent) the page tells the app its language as it opens and at each pick (`PUT /api/desktop/lang`); another browser never does.
- `e2e/specs/mobile.spec.ts` — a phone's screen: the sidebar from the menu, sending by touch, a conversation's pin shown without hover and pinned by a tap, a row added to a CSV file from Files by touch, the header's actions under ⋯, no sideways scrolling; at 320px and 280px, a template's editor, the team settings, a new schedule of each kind (in both languages, English also at 400px and 430px and Chinese at 250px and 360px: the selected trigger visible in the When menu, and the rows the week's days take; two things to wait for, each in its box with its Remove; a saved kind at the row's end in view when the drawer opens again) and a chat's Schedule without sideways scrolling, and the add card listing every CLI in one column; the home page's tagline smaller with each line unwrapped, and the logo closing the sidebar; scrolled up, the arrow to the end in the middle above the message box, a finger's target larger than it, and a tap taking the chat there with the keyboard left as it was (the message box's focus neither taken nor given).

The fakes check Harness Relay's side of each protocol, not the CLIs' own: a CLI that changes what it writes is caught by the adapter tests' recorded messages and by the [live tests](#live-tests), which run the real CLIs. Nor do they run the CLIs' own sandboxes: the fakes of Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Copilot and Muse Code work out what theirs would allow, while the open agents' fakes run in Harness Relay's own sandbox for real; that the real CLIs below full access write nothing outside the chat's folders is a live test (`e2e/live/sandbox.spec.ts`).

## Live tests

```bash
E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA=http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1 npm run test:e2e:live
E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA=http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1 E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA_TYPED=gemma4:26b E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1 npm run test:e2e:live
E2E_LIVE_VLLM=http://127.0.0.1:8000/v1 E2E_LIVE_VLLM_MODEL=<its served name> E2E_LIVE_VLLM_KEY=<its key> npm run test:e2e:live
E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA=… E2E_LIVE_CLIS=opencode,claude npx playwright test -c playwright.live.config.ts -g "Team settings"
GEMINI_API_KEY=<your key> E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1 E2E_LIVE_CLIS=antigravity E2E_LIVE_MODEL_ANTIGRAVITY=gemini-3.8-flash npm run test:e2e:live
npx playwright show-report playwright-report-live
```

The same server and UI with the real CLIs, signed in as you are, on real models: an Ollama or vLLM server of your own (any OpenAI-compatible endpoint), and with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1` the CLIs' own cloud models, which your sign-ins pay for. They never run on CI: each test is skipped unless the environment names what it needs. Run them on each system you can when you change an adapter, the sandboxes or the model backends; they find what the fakes cannot (a CLI that only runs with full access on one system, or writes somewhere without asking). One test at a time ([`playwright.live.config.ts`](../playwright.live.config.ts): one worker, up to 20 minutes a test, the report in `playwright-report-live/`), since a turn on a real model is slow and the CLIs share your own folders.

- **What they run with** (`startRelay({ live: true })`, through `test.use({ live: true })`): each test still has a server of its own, with its own data folder, working directories and `RELAY_KEYRING=off` (an endpoint's key stays in that test's database, encrypted), but `HOME` and each CLI's own folder are yours, so the CLIs run with your sign-ins and their sessions land in your folders; no fake comes first on the `PATH`, and no CLI is set up to be missing. A CLI that is not on the `PATH` is named by `E2E_LIVE_BIN_<PROVIDER>` (`E2E_LIVE_BIN_OPENHANDS=~/.local/bin/openhands`). On macOS and Linux the server's folder is in `/tmp`: Harness Relay's own sandbox needs a short path for its sockets, and macOS's temporary folder is too deep. Since the CLIs run as you, an approval is given only for what the tests ask (`liveAllows()`: a command that writes `hi` into a file, a write in the chat's own folders); anything else is refused. What any run of Harness Relay leaves in the CLIs' own folders stays there: the endpoints' models block in Grok's `config.toml`, the CLIs' sessions, and the trust a CLI records for a project folder (here temporary ones).
- **The environment**: `E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA` (a base URL ending in `/v1`) and `E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA_MODEL` (default `gemma4:e4b`), `E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA_TYPED` (another model that server has, for a model typed for the endpoint), `E2E_LIVE_VLLM`, `E2E_LIVE_VLLM_MODEL` and `E2E_LIVE_VLLM_KEY`, `E2E_LIVE_CLIS` (the CLIs to try, comma-separated; default all twelve), `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1` (also the CLIs' own cloud models: Claude Code's, Codex's, Grok's, Cursor's, Antigravity's, Copilot's and Muse Code's, `CLOUD_CLIS` in `live.ts`) and `E2E_LIVE_MODEL_<CLI>` (a model of that CLI's own list, picked in Team settings for its cloud tests, such as `E2E_LIVE_MODEL_ANTIGRAVITY=gemini-3.8-flash`; unset, its default model). Each test sets the endpoints up in its server's Settings, and lets OpenHands use a terminal in the sandbox, which it needs below full access. Antigravity has no custom endpoints, so it runs only with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`, and only with `GEMINI_API_KEY` in your environment, which live runs keep (`has()` in `live.ts`): Harness Relay never runs it on the Google sign-in agy keeps. Copilot runs on an endpoint through its BYOK variables, with no GitHub sign-in, and on its own models with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`, signed in as it keeps it (with GitHub, in your `~/.copilot` and the system's credential store) or with `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN` or `GITHUB_TOKEN` in your environment. Neither has Muse Code: it runs only with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`, signed in as muse keeps it (with a Meta account, in your `~/.config/muse` and, on macOS, the login keychain) or with `META_API_KEY` in your environment. Muse Code was checked only with a fake Meta API key against a stand-in for the Meta API: its live tests have not been run on real models yet.
- **What they check** ([`e2e/live/`](../e2e/live/), the helpers in `live.ts`):
  - `models.spec.ts` — each CLI that runs on an endpoint (Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Copilot and the five open agents) on each endpoint given: its model picked in Team settings from the model menu, which lists it, saved, and a question answered (17 + 25), with Claude Code's, Codex's, Grok's and Copilot's session recorded on that endpoint (`agent_sessions.backend`, read from the test's database: not their own service); the same four with the endpoint's model as their default model, picked on their card in Settings under **Your own endpoints**, for a member without a model of its own; with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`, each of `CLOUD_CLIS` on its own default model, and on `E2E_LIVE_MODEL_<CLI>` picked in Team settings when it is set (Claude Code's, Codex's, Grok's and Copilot's session recorded on their own service); a member on a model of a deleted endpoint failing its turn with the reason (the first of Claude Code, Codex, Grok and Copilot in `E2E_LIVE_CLIS`: the endpoint was deleted and the turn did not run; the first open agent: there is no such endpoint); Grok on a model typed for the endpoint that its list leaves out (`E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA_TYPED`); on Windows, an open agent below full access not running, and saying why.
  - `teams.spec.ts` — one team of up to three CLIs on their own models (Claude Code first) and two on the endpoints, each answering a question of its own (`E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`); each CLI writing a file in each of two working directories through its shell, in edit mode, on the first endpoint's model, and with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1` each of `CLOUD_CLIS` on its own model too (Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Copilot and Muse Code in their own sandboxes, Cursor and Antigravity asking before each command; the open agents on Windows and OpenCode on Linux with full access, the only access they run with there); Cursor writing in the second one with its own file tool, its card saying the file is in another of the chat's working directories (in folders under your home, removed afterwards, since Cursor writes in a temporary folder without asking); Antigravity, with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`, ending its turn at a refused command (`echo no >`, which `liveAllows()` refuses: no file, the refused call says that Antigravity ends its turn after a refusal, and nothing follows it), and asking about a project's own MCP server (`.agents/mcp_config.json`) before agy starts: refused, the turn does not start and the server never runs; Copilot, on the first endpoint's model or with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1` on its own, ending its turn at a refused command (`echo no >` in a read-only chat, which it asks about and `liveAllows()` refuses: no file, the refused call says that Copilot ends its turn after a refusal, and nothing follows it), and asking about a project's own hook (a `sessionStart` hook in `.github/hooks/`) before it starts: refused, the turn does not start and the hook never runs.
  - `sandbox.spec.ts` — what the fakes cannot show: each CLI that runs below full access here, asked in edit mode to write a file outside the chat's folders (`echo hi >` into a folder under your home, not a temporary one), with the chat refusing on its own (`approvals: 'deny'`), writes nothing there: Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Copilot and Muse Code kept in by their own sandboxes, the open agents by Harness Relay's, and Cursor and Antigravity, which have none, by asking. On an endpoint's model, or the CLI's own with `E2E_LIVE_CLOUD=1`; skipped when the model did not even try.
- A small model may not do what it is asked (call its shell tool, answer with the number alone), so a failed check says what the turn did: its error, and the tools it called. `E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA_MODEL` picks a stronger one.

## Continuous integration

[`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs on every pull request and every push to `main`: `npm ci`, `npm run format:check`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` and `npm run build`, on Ubuntu with Node 22 (the oldest supported) and Node 24, and on Windows (`windows-latest`) with Node 24 (for a pull request, when it touches a file the tests depend on: see below), where the tests' temporary folder is on `D:` with the checkout (`runner.temp`) rather than on `C:`. On Ubuntu it also installs the desktop app's tools (without the Electron binary), typechecks the desktop app, builds it (`desktop/scripts/build.ts`) and runs its bundled server as the app does (`desktop/scripts/smoke.ts`). A second job (`desktop-package`) packages the desktop app without an installer, for Linux (`electron-builder --linux dir`, for x64 and on GitHub's Arm runner for Arm64 as a release does) and for Windows x64 (`--win dir`; Windows on Arm is checked by the Release workflow), and checks it as a release is checked (`desktop/scripts/check-package.ts`: on Linux on a virtual display, with Electron's own libraries installed where the runner lacks them), so on every pull request that changes what the app is made of, the packaged app has a team of the fake CLIs answer a question on Linux and Windows. That job builds the app with a made-up `RELAY_UPDATES_TOKEN`, put together in the shell, which the check must then find in the app, encoded only. Releases have a workflow of their own: see [Releasing](#releasing). A new push to a pull request cancels its previous run. Run the same commands locally before pushing.

[`.github/workflows/e2e.yml`](../.github/workflows/e2e.yml) runs the [end-to-end tests](#end-to-end-tests) (`npm run test:e2e`, Chromium) on Ubuntu, macOS and Windows with Node 24, and on Ubuntu with Node 22 too (each in two jobs, half the tests each, `--shard=1/2` and `2/2`; up to 45 minutes a job): for every release candidate, which the [Release workflow](#releasing) publishes only once they pass (it calls this workflow, `workflow_call`), and for each pull request that touches a file they depend on (see below), but not one a maintainer opened (`MAINTAINERS` in `scripts/ci-scope.ts`: their changes are tested by the release candidate's run, and every release comes after one). They do not run for a push to `main`. Run it by hand (**Actions → End-to-end tests → Run workflow**, on any branch) to choose other browsers, or to try a branch of your own before its release candidate. Before the tests it sets up Harness Relay's own sandbox, which the open agents' fakes run in below full access: on Ubuntu it installs bubblewrap, socat and ripgrep where the runner lacks them and, where Ubuntu restricts unprivileged user namespaces, an AppArmor profile that lets `/usr/bin/bwrap` make them; on Ubuntu it then runs the sandbox's own checks (`npx tsx scripts/sandbox-check.ts`; not on macOS, whose runners' curl, on macOS 26.7, cannot verify a certificate inside the sandbox, so the checks that reach example.com fail there), and on Ubuntu and macOS the tests with `E2E_REQUIRE_RELAY_SANDBOX=1`, so a test that needs the sandbox fails there rather than skips. It runs them with `E2E_SYSTEM_CLIS=1`: each CLI's own tests for all twelve on Ubuntu, and on macOS and Windows for the CLIs Harness Relay runs differently there ([e2e/README.md, Platforms and browsers](../e2e/README.md#platforms-and-browsers)); run it by hand with **every_cli** for all twelve everywhere. Each job uploads the HTML report, with the trace, screenshot, video and logs of each failed test (`e2e-report-<os>-node<version>-part<1 or 2>-attempt<N>`, the attempt so that a job run again uploads beside the report of the one that failed).

**What a pull request runs.** The first job of each workflow (**What the change needs**) runs [`scripts/ci-scope.ts`](../scripts/ci-scope.ts), which gates the slower jobs on a pull request: the tests on Windows and the packaged desktop app in `ci.yml`, the end-to-end tests in `e2e.yml`; the run's summary says which run and why. The end-to-end tests also go by who opened the pull request (`PR_AUTHOR`, from the event): none for a maintainer's (`MAINTAINERS`, GitHub logins: `cyyeh`), whatever it touches, the script itself included (`e2eFor()`); anyone else's runs them as its files say. Each lists the files it is known not to depend on (`IGNORES`), never the ones it needs: it runs unless every file the pull request touches is on its list, so a file or folder no list names runs everything. A change to the docs, the landing page or the dev container alone runs none of them; to the unit tests alone, the tests on Windows only; to the desktop app alone, all but the end-to-end tests. Every push to `main` runs everything but the end-to-end tests (they run for the release candidate), so that `main` is checked whole after each merge, also where the lists miss a file; so does a run by hand (everything, the end-to-end tests included, as for the Release workflow's call), and a pull request whose checkout is not its merge commit, that changes no files or the script itself, or whose change the script cannot tell. The Linux checks (`check`) are not gated: `npm test` also reads the docs, the landing page, the issue forms, the dev container and the workflows, so every change is theirs. A pull request's change is its merge commit against the base it merges into, a rename counting as a deletion and an addition. `node scripts/ci-scope.ts [<base>]` tells you locally what your branch's commits would run as a pull request (since `origin/main` by default, by `PR_AUTHOR` when you set it; with Node 22.18 or later, which runs TypeScript itself, else `npx tsx scripts/ci-scope.ts`). When a gated job comes to read, import or ship a file on its list, take the file off the list: `test/ci-scope.test.ts` follows the imports of the end-to-end tests and the packaged app and fails for one on their lists, and for a pattern that names no file, but it cannot see a file read at run time (as `desktop/scripts/build.ts` copies `LICENSE`).

Each workflow ends in one check that stands for all its jobs, **CI passed** and **End-to-end passed**: a job the change did not need counts as passed (the end-to-end tests of a maintainer's pull request too), one that failed or was cancelled fails it. To block merging until the checks pass, require those in a ruleset (or branch protection rule) for `main` in the repository settings, rather than the jobs' own checks: a skipped matrix job reports one check under its name unexpanded, never one per entry, so a ruleset that requires an entry's check waits for it forever.

[`.github/workflows/cli-versions.yml`](../.github/workflows/cli-versions.yml) runs every day (and by hand) on this repository only: layer 1 of `scripts/cli-check.ts`, which compares each agent CLI's newest version with the one it was checked with, then checks each new version on Linux against the version checked, once, and opens an issue labelled `cli-check` only when that check found a failed check or something removed, posting the report there. A clean check is remembered, from the previous successful run that was not a pull request, and opens none; a pull request that changes the check runs those checks for every CLI and posts nothing. A source that names an older version than the one remembered, or than an open issue tells of, does not close that issue or replace the remembered version. See [Checking a CLI's new version](#checking-a-clis-new-version), "On CI".

### Website

The Tools pages in both languages are the source for the app’s bundled OAuth guides. The Vite plugin in `scripts/oauth-guides.ts` packages `#google-oauth-setup` and `#microsoft-oauth-setup` with their screenshots as `/help/oauth/<lang>/<provider>.html`. Editing those sections updates the in-app help. Keep both IDs, section headings and the page canonical URL so the build can generate all four guides.

Settings → Publish to the cloud links the publishing guides on the live site instead (`src/web/siteDocs.ts`): `#render-setup` and `#neon-setup` on `docs/conversations/`, in each language. Released apps keep those links, so keep both ids; `test/siteDocs.test.ts` checks that each link leads to a section of its page. Their pictures blur everything personal before they are taken (site/DESIGN-GUIDELINES.md, "Assets").

The landing page at https://agentic-system-lab.github.io/harness-relay/ is plain HTML, CSS and JavaScript in [`site/`](../site/): `index.html` (English), `zh-TW/index.html` (繁體中文), the use-cases page in each language (`use-cases/index.html`, `zh-TW/use-cases/index.html`, whose recordings `npm run use-cases` makes: [Recording the use cases](#recording-the-use-cases)), the viewer a share's link opens (`share/index.html`, one page in both languages, not one of the site's: [Architecture → Sharing a conversation](architecture.md#sharing-a-conversation)), the docs for people who use the app, a page per task in each language (`docs/index.html` and `docs/<page>/index.html`, `zh-TW/docs/…`, their pictures from the app and the use cases’ recordings in `assets/media/docs/`; the technical details stay in this repository's `docs/`, which each page links at its end) and shared `assets/`. There is no build step: the docs are written by hand like the rest, and `test/site-docs.test.ts` keeps what their pages share the same and every link of theirs going somewhere that is there. Search reads each docs page's HTML in the browser (`site/assets/docs.js`), and a button on every page copies that page as Markdown; both stay hidden without JavaScript, so a page still reads with scripts off. A new docs page goes in `DOC_SLUGS` (`test/site-pages.ts`), every page's menu, its neighbours' previous and next links, and the sitemap. [`.github/workflows/pages.yml`](../.github/workflows/pages.yml) runs on pushes to `main` that touch `site/`, `docs/media/` or the workflow itself: it copies `site/` and the demo videos from `docs/media/` into one folder, leaves out Markdown files, dates the pages in the sitemap, and deploys it to GitHub Pages (repository settings → Pages → Source: GitHub Actions), then tells IndexNow which pages the deployment changed (below). It does this in two jobs: one builds the site and uploads it, the other deploys it, and tries a second time a minute later when the first try fails (GitHub may list an upload only a while after it is made). A deployment that failed both times can run again on its own with **Re-run failed jobs**, which deploys the site the build job uploaded. **Re-run all jobs** would upload the site a second time, and the deployment would then fail, since `deploy-pages` refuses a run with two uploads of it: start a new run instead (Actions → Pages → Run workflow). Preview it with `npm run site:preview` ([`scripts/preview-site.ts`](../scripts/preview-site.ts)): it serves `site/` on http://127.0.0.1:4180 (`SITE_PORT` changes the port) with the videos read from `docs/media/`, the way Pages will. Read [`site/DESIGN-GUIDELINES.md`](../site/DESIGN-GUIDELINES.md) before changing the page; it also says what to update when the demo is re-recorded.

**Search engines.** [`site/sitemap.xml`](../site/sitemap.xml) lists every page (the landing page, the use-cases page and the docs' pages, in each language, in the order `test/site-pages.ts` gives them) at its canonical address, each with the same `hreflang` alternates as its `<head>` (`test/site-sitemap.test.ts` keeps them in step): add a page to it, and to every page's alternates, when the site gets one. It has no `<lastmod>` of its own: the Pages workflow adds each page's when it deploys, the time, in UTC, of the last commit that changed the page's HTML (it checks out the whole history for that, without the files' old contents), and Google and Bing read it to know when a page changed (Google takes no part in IndexNow, below: the date is what tells it). A shallow checkout, which would date every page at its one commit, and a page no commit has go out without one, with a warning. Crawlers find it through [the `robots.txt` at the domain's root](https://agentic-system-lab.github.io/robots.txt), which names it and lets every crawler read every page. Crawlers read a `robots.txt` only at a host's root, so that one is not this repository's: the org's homepage repository (`agentic-system-lab.github.io`) publishes it, and when the sitemap moves or is renamed, its `Sitemap:` line there changes too. The sitemap is also submitted in Google Search Console, under the URL-prefix property `https://agentic-system-lab.github.io/harness-relay/`, and in Bing Webmaster Tools, which imported that property and its sitemap from Search Console; Bing's AI Performance report there shows how often Copilot cites the pages. The viewer of shared conversations (`share/`) is kept out: `noindex`, and in no sitemap, since a conversation is for those it was given to. The site has no other pages to keep out (nothing links to the verification file, the IndexNow key file or the font's license, and a missing address answers 404). `site/google7b263ecdf9adc06e.html` is the file that proves the property ours: Google checks it again from time to time, so keep it as Google wrote it, and never delete it. Bing has no file of its own: it checks through Search Console, from time to time, that the property is still verified there, so the site stays verified in Bing only while it is in Google.

**IndexNow.** After each deployment the Pages workflow tells [IndexNow](https://www.indexnow.org) which pages it added, changed or removed, so that Bing reads them again soon rather than at its next crawl (`api.indexnow.org` passes them on to every engine that takes part; Google takes none, and learns of a change from the sitemap's `<lastmod>`). Before it deploys, it compares each page of the new sitemap with the one live (a page it cannot read there counts as changed) and the live sitemap with the new one (a page only the live one lists was removed); once the site is out, it sends those pages' addresses, and nothing when no page changed. The key, `INDEXNOW_KEY` in the workflow, is not a secret: `site/<key>.txt` serves it at the top of the site, below the domain's root, which makes it good only for the pages under `/harness-relay/`, all of the site's. To change it, rename that file and change the workflow's key with it (`test/site-indexnow.test.ts` checks that they match); never delete the file while the workflow names its key, or IndexNow refuses the pages (403). A failure only warns, in the run's log and summary: the site is out, and the engines find the change when they next read the sitemap, whose `<lastmod>` dates it. Bing Webmaster Tools → IndexNow lists the pages Bing was told of.

**Answer engines.** AI search (Google's AI Overviews, ChatGPT search, Perplexity, Copilot) cites pages it finds through the ordinary indexes, so the page's own text, title and description come first; nothing on the page needs JavaScript to be read, which matters as most AI crawlers do not run it. Beyond them, each home page's `<head>` has one JSON-LD graph ([schema.org](https://schema.org)): the page (`WebPage`), the site (`WebSite`), its publisher (`Organization`, the org's homepage, under the `@id` the homepage's own JSON-LD gives it, so that both sites describe one organization: keep it), the app (`SoftwareApplication`: systems, newest release, the license by name), and its repository (`SoftwareSourceCode`); each use-cases page's, the page and one `VideoObject` for each use case's recording (written by `npm run use-cases`), the site and the app referred to by the landing page's `@id`s. It says only what the page says, in the page's language, and names what its links lead to (the repository, its LICENSE, the org's homepage as it names itself); never a version, a size, a price, a count, a rating or a review. `test/site-structured-data.test.ts` keeps it in step with each page's `<head>` and markup, and the two pages with each other, and allows only the properties it checks: change them with the page, and a new property with a check of its own. Google shows no rich result for it, and Search Console may list the app as an invalid software app (Google's needs a rating or review, and a price). This warning is expected. [`site/llms.txt`](../site/llms.txt) ([llmstxt.org](https://llmstxt.org)) sums the app up for the coding agents and tools that read one (most AI crawlers do not, and Google Search does not use it) and links the docs as Markdown on `main`; its facts are the page's and the code's (the test checks the agents, the systems, the Node.js version, the address it listens on and where Harness Relay's own sandbox does not run), and a user-facing doc that is added goes in it. The `llms.txt` at the domain's root, the homepage's, links it. The meta description starts with the name and fits in a search result (160 columns); `og:description` keeps the longer one for link previews.

### Recording the demo

The demo in the READMEs and the first-chat guides (`docs/media/demo-*.mp4` and `.webp`) is a real run, recorded by `npm run demo` ([`scripts/demo/`](../scripts/demo/)). Record it again when the UI it passes through changes. It needs the three CLIs installed and signed in, FFmpeg and libwebp (`brew install ffmpeg webp` on macOS), and the Chromium that `playwright-core` drives (`npx playwright-core install chromium-headless-shell` once, or `DEMO_CHROME` set to a Chrome or Chromium binary).

```bash
npm run demo                          # record both languages, cut them and publish them
npm run demo -- record --dry          # check the form up to submission without running agents (under a minute)
npm run demo -- record --lang en      # one step, one language
npm run demo -- cut publish           # cut and publish the last recordings again
```

1. **record** builds the UI and starts Harness Relay on its own port (8791, and 8792 for the second language) with an empty data folder in `data/demo/<lang>/`, so your own conversations and settings are left alone; this repository is the chat's working directory, read-only. A headless browser then does what a person would, with a drawn cursor: a new group, the Three-way debate template, a new chat (with lead review unticked, since the chapters and stills show no review) and a question that names all three CLIs, the lead agent's team (confirmed), the answers (a request beyond read-only is shown and declined, a question the agents ask is skipped), a follow-up question with a team of its own, Files at relay mode's hand-off in `orchestrator.ts`, and a search. Both languages record at once, about half an hour with the real CLIs. Each step leaves a mark in `marks.json`.
2. **cut** speeds the agents' work up so the video lasts about two minutes (`--length`), and writes `video.mp4` (1600×1000, 24 fps), `animation.webp` (880×550, 8 fps) and `timeline.json`. If the recorder waited for nothing, add a `skip-start` / `skip-end` pair to `marks.json` and cut again.
3. **publish** copies the video and the animation into `docs/media/`, re-cuts the posters, the screenshots and the social cards from the frame where the first team's card shows (the `poster` mark), re-times the frame times in `site/DESIGN-GUIDELINES.md`, and writes `data/demo/<lang>/review.md`: the chapters as written, the teams, any approval, the search term and the replies.

Then read `review.md` and watch the videos. The chapter sentences are in `scripts/demo/chapters.ts`; the teams, names, rounds, the group and the search term are filled in from the recording, but when the agents did something the sentences don't describe, change them there and run `npm run demo -- publish`. Check the READMEs' captions and the first-chat guides’ video links too, then commit the media, `site/` and the scripts together.

### Recording the use cases
